Agent Session Logs — Setup Guide
Hand this file to your coding agent (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, etc.) and ask it to set up session logging in the current repo. The end result: every prompt + final response from the session is captured to .claude-logs/ in the directory where the session started, and committed alongside the work — but none of the intermediate tool calls, file reads, or work artefacts are recorded.

What this does
On every prompt submit and at the end of every turn, a hook runs a small Python script.
The script reads the agent's own session transcript (the JSONL file the agent already writes for itself) and produces a clean markdown digest with frontmatter stats.
Output is written to <session-cwd>/.claude-logs/<timestamp>_<session-id>.md — so each repo's sessions land in that repo's own log directory.
.claude-logs/ is the canonical directory regardless of which agent you're using (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Aider, etc.). One repo, one log location, one commit policy. Don't pick a per-agent name like .codex-logs/ — collapse everything into .claude-logs/ so reviewers and tooling have one place to look.
Only user prompts and final assistant text are kept. Tool calls, tool results, file diffs, thinking blocks, and sidechain agent output are stripped.
Each run overwrites the same session file (idempotent), so there's only ever one log per session.
Each log records who submitted it: git author name, git email, and the authenticated GitHub login (via gh api user). This survives in the committed file's frontmatter, so the log itself answers "whose session was this" without needing to inspect the commit.
The intent is "what did I ask, what did the agent say back" — a conversation transcript, not an audit log of operations.

Agent instructions (read this carefully)
You are being asked to install session logging. Do the following without modifying any other settings:

Confirm the platform. This guide is written for Claude Code's hook system + JSONL session format (~/.claude/projects/<dashed-cwd>/*.jsonl). If the user is running a different agent (Codex, Cursor, Aider, etc.), see the "Other agents" section at the bottom — adapt the script to read that agent's transcript format, but keep the output format and .claude-logs/ location identical.

Write ~/.claude/extract-log.py with the exact contents from the "extract-log.py" section below. Make it executable: chmod +x ~/.claude/extract-log.py.

Merge the hooks in the "settings.json hooks" section into ~/.claude/settings.json. Do not overwrite the file — read it, merge the hooks.UserPromptSubmit and hooks.Stop entries (preserving anything already there), and write it back.

Add .claude-logs/ to the repo's commit policy, not its .gitignore. These logs are meant to be committed. If the user has a project CLAUDE.md, add the line: Always commit \.claude-logs/` with your changes.`

Verify. Trigger the hook once (any prompt will do), then check that .claude-logs/<today>_<session>.md exists in the current working directory and contains a [CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=PROMPT ...] block followed by the prompt text.

Do not log anything else. Do not add hooks that capture tool calls, bash output, or file diffs. The whole point is that the work product stays out of the log.

extract-log.py
Save to ~/.claude/extract-log.py:

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Extract clean conversation logs from Claude Code sessions."""

import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime
from typing import List, Optional, Tuple, Dict, Any


def get_git_author() -> str:
    """Get the author for attribution. Checks CLAUDE_AUTHOR env var first, then git config."""
    if author := os.environ.get('CLAUDE_AUTHOR'):
        return author
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(
            ['git', 'config', 'user.name'],
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            timeout=5
        )
        if result.returncode == 0 and result.stdout.strip():
            return result.stdout.strip()
    except (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, FileNotFoundError):
        pass
    return 'unknown'


def get_github_user() -> str:
    """Get the authenticated GitHub login via `gh`. Returns 'unknown' if unavailable."""
    if login := os.environ.get('GITHUB_USER'):
        return login
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(
            ['gh', 'api', 'user', '--jq', '.login'],
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            timeout=5
        )
        if result.returncode == 0 and result.stdout.strip():
            return result.stdout.strip()
    except (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, FileNotFoundError):
        pass
    return 'unknown'


def get_git_email() -> str:
    """Get the git config user.email for attribution."""
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(
            ['git', 'config', 'user.email'],
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            timeout=5
        )
        if result.returncode == 0 and result.stdout.strip():
            return result.stdout.strip()
    except (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, FileNotFoundError):
        pass
    return 'unknown'


def get_project_session_dir(cwd: Path) -> Path:
    """Get the session directory for a project."""
    claude_dir = Path.home() / '.claude' / 'projects'
    project_hash = str(cwd).replace('/', '-')
    return claude_dir / project_hash


def is_user_prompt(entry: dict) -> bool:
    """Check if entry is a real user prompt (not meta/command/tool result)."""
    if entry.get('type') != 'user':
        return False
    if entry.get('isMeta') or entry.get('isSidechain'):
        return False

    content = entry.get('message', {}).get('content', '')

    if isinstance(content, list):
        if any(c.get('type') == 'tool_result' for c in content):
            return False
        return False

    if isinstance(content, str):
        if content.startswith('<command-name>'):
            return False
        if content.startswith('<local-command'):
            return False
        if content.startswith('Caveat:'):
            return False
        if not content.strip():
            return False
        return True

    return False


def extract_assistant_text(entry: dict) -> Optional[str]:
    """Extract text content from assistant message."""
    if entry.get('type') != 'assistant':
        return None
    if entry.get('isSidechain'):
        return None

    content = entry.get('message', {}).get('content', [])

    if isinstance(content, str):
        return content if content.strip() else None

    if isinstance(content, list):
        texts = []
        for block in content:
            if block.get('type') == 'text':
                text = block.get('text', '').strip()
                if text:
                    texts.append(text)
        return '\n\n'.join(texts) if texts else None

    return None


def extract_clean_log(jsonl_path: Path) -> List[Tuple[str, str, str]]:
    """Extract conversation entries from a JSONL file."""
    entries = []

    for line in jsonl_path.read_text().strip().split('\n'):
        if not line.strip():
            continue
        try:
            entry = json.loads(line)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            continue

        timestamp = entry.get('timestamp', '')

        if is_user_prompt(entry):
            content = entry.get('message', {}).get('content', '')
            if isinstance(content, str) and content.strip():
                entries.append((timestamp, 'user', content.strip()))

        elif entry.get('type') == 'assistant':
            text = extract_assistant_text(entry)
            if text:
                entries.append((timestamp, 'assistant', text))

    return entries


def format_timestamp(iso_timestamp: str) -> str:
    try:
        dt = datetime.fromisoformat(iso_timestamp.replace('Z', '+00:00'))
        return dt.strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M')
    except (ValueError, AttributeError):
        return ''


def dedupe_entries(entries: List[Tuple[str, str, str]]) -> List[Tuple[str, str, str]]:
    """Keep only the final assistant response before each user prompt."""
    if not entries:
        return []

    result = []
    pending_assistant = None

    for timestamp, role, content in entries:
        if role == 'user':
            if pending_assistant:
                result.append(pending_assistant)
                pending_assistant = None
            result.append((timestamp, role, content))
        elif role == 'assistant':
            pending_assistant = (timestamp, role, content)

    if pending_assistant:
        result.append(pending_assistant)

    return result


def detect_paste_indicators(content: str) -> Dict[str, bool]:
    """Detect heuristic indicators that content was pasted."""
    indicators = {
        'has_code_block': '```' in content,
        'has_file_paths': bool(re.search(r'[\w\-/]+\.(ts|js|py|tsx|jsx|json|md|txt|yml|yaml):\d+', content)) or
                         bool(re.search(r'(?:^|[\s\(])/[\w\-/]+\.[\w]+', content, re.MULTILINE)),
        'has_urls': bool(re.search(r'https?://', content)),
        'is_long': len(content) > 500,
        'has_stack_trace': bool(re.search(r'at \w+.*:\d+:\d+', content)) or
                          bool(re.search(r'File ".*", line \d+', content)),
        'has_structured_data': bool(re.search(r'^[\s]*[{\[]', content, re.MULTILINE)) and
                              ('{' in content and '}' in content),
    }

    indicator_count = sum(indicators.values())
    indicators['likely_pasted'] = indicator_count >= 2 or indicators['is_long']

    return indicators


def parse_iso_timestamp(timestamp: str) -> Optional[datetime]:
    try:
        return datetime.fromisoformat(timestamp.replace('Z', '+00:00'))
    except (ValueError, AttributeError):
        return None


def calculate_session_stats(entries: List[Tuple[str, str, str]]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Calculate comprehensive session statistics."""
    if not entries:
        return {}

    prompts = [(ts, content) for ts, role, content in entries if role == 'user']
    responses = [(ts, content) for ts, role, content in entries if role == 'assistant']

    if not prompts:
        return {}

    stats = {}

    first_prompt_dt = parse_iso_timestamp(prompts[0][0])
    last_prompt_dt = parse_iso_timestamp(prompts[-1][0])

    stats['first_prompt_time'] = prompts[0][0]
    stats['last_prompt_time'] = prompts[-1][0]

    if first_prompt_dt and last_prompt_dt:
        duration = (last_prompt_dt - first_prompt_dt).total_seconds() / 60
        stats['session_duration_minutes'] = round(duration, 1)

        if len(prompts) > 1:
            avg_gap = duration / (len(prompts) - 1)
            stats['avg_time_between_prompts_minutes'] = round(avg_gap, 1)

    prompt_chars = [len(content) for _, content in prompts]
    prompt_words = [len(content.split()) for _, content in prompts]

    stats['total_prompt_chars'] = sum(prompt_chars)
    stats['total_prompt_words'] = sum(prompt_words)
    stats['avg_prompt_length_chars'] = round(sum(prompt_chars) / len(prompts), 1)
    stats['avg_prompt_length_words'] = round(sum(prompt_words) / len(prompts), 1)
    stats['longest_prompt_words'] = max(prompt_words)
    stats['shortest_prompt_words'] = min(prompt_words)

    if responses:
        response_chars = [len(content) for _, content in responses]
        stats['total_response_chars'] = sum(response_chars)
        stats['avg_response_length_chars'] = round(sum(response_chars) / len(responses), 1)

        if stats['total_prompt_chars'] > 0:
            stats['response_to_prompt_ratio'] = round(
                stats['total_response_chars'] / stats['total_prompt_chars'], 2
            )

    paste_data = [detect_paste_indicators(content) for _, content in prompts]
    stats['prompts_with_code_blocks'] = sum(1 for p in paste_data if p['has_code_block'])
    stats['prompts_with_file_paths'] = sum(1 for p in paste_data if p['has_file_paths'])
    stats['prompts_with_urls'] = sum(1 for p in paste_data if p['has_urls'])
    stats['prompts_with_long_content'] = sum(1 for p in paste_data if p['is_long'])
    stats['likely_pasted_count'] = sum(1 for p in paste_data if p['likely_pasted'])

    return stats


def main():
    cwd = Path(os.environ.get('CLAUDE_CWD', os.getcwd()))
    session_dir = get_project_session_dir(cwd)

    if not session_dir.exists():
        return

    session_files = [
        f for f in session_dir.glob('*.jsonl')
        if not f.name.startswith('agent-')
    ]

    if not session_files:
        return

    latest = max(session_files, key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime)
    session_id = latest.stem

    entries = extract_clean_log(latest)
    entries = dedupe_entries(entries)

    if not entries:
        return

    session_stats = calculate_session_stats(entries)

    output_dir = cwd / '.claude-logs'
    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    existing_files = list(output_dir.glob(f"*_{session_id}.md"))
    date = datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d')
    if existing_files:
        output_path = existing_files[0]
    else:
        timestamp = datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d_%H-%M-%S')
        filename = f"{timestamp}_{session_id}.md"
        output_path = output_dir / filename

    last_entry_type = entries[-1][1] if entries else 'unknown'
    session_status = 'complete' if last_entry_type == 'assistant' else 'awaiting_response'

    author = get_git_author()
    github_user = get_github_user()
    git_email = get_git_email()
    lines = ["---"]
    lines.append(f"session_id: {session_id}")
    lines.append(f"date: {date}")
    lines.append(f"author: {author}")
    lines.append(f"github_user: {github_user}")
    lines.append(f"git_email: {git_email}")
    lines.append(f"project: {cwd.name}")
    lines.append(f"session_status: {session_status}")
    lines.append(f"last_entry_type: {last_entry_type}")
    lines.append(f"total_exchanges: {len([e for e in entries if e[1] == 'user'])}")
    lines.append(f"generated_at: {datetime.now().isoformat()}")

    for key, value in session_stats.items():
        lines.append(f"{key}: {value}")

    lines.append("---\n")
    lines.append(f"# Claude Session Log - {date}\n")
    lines.append(f"Session: `{session_id}` | Project: `{cwd.name}` | Author: `{author}` | GitHub: `{github_user}`\n")
    lines.append("---\n")

    prompts_data = []
    for timestamp, role, content in entries:
        if role == 'user':
            paste_indicators = detect_paste_indicators(content)
            prompts_data.append({
                'timestamp': timestamp,
                'content': content,
                'chars': len(content),
                'words': len(content.split()),
                'paste_indicators': paste_indicators,
            })

    exchange_num = 0
    prompt_index = 0

    for i, (timestamp, role, content) in enumerate(entries):
        time_str = format_timestamp(timestamp)

        if role == 'user':
            exchange_num += 1
            prompt_data = prompts_data[prompt_index]
            indicators = prompt_data['paste_indicators']

            lines.append(f"\n[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=PROMPT num={exchange_num} session={session_id}]")
            lines.append(f"timestamp: {timestamp}")
            lines.append(f"time: {time_str}")
            lines.append(f"chars: {prompt_data['chars']}")
            lines.append(f"words: {prompt_data['words']}")
            lines.append(f"has_code_block: {indicators['has_code_block']}")
            lines.append(f"has_file_paths: {indicators['has_file_paths']}")
            lines.append(f"has_urls: {indicators['has_urls']}")
            lines.append(f"likely_pasted: {indicators['likely_pasted']}")
            lines.append(f"\n{content}\n")

            prompt_index += 1
        else:
            lines.append(f"\n[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num={exchange_num} session={session_id}]")
            lines.append(f"timestamp: {timestamp}")
            lines.append(f"time: {time_str}")
            lines.append(f"chars: {len(content)}")
            lines.append(f"\n{content}\n")

    output_path.write_text('\n'.join(lines))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
settings.json hooks
Merge into ~/.claude/settings.json (preserving any existing hooks):

{
  "hooks": {
    "UserPromptSubmit": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          { "type": "command", "command": "python3 ~/.claude/extract-log.py" }
        ]
      }
    ],
    "Stop": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          { "type": "command", "command": "python3 ~/.claude/extract-log.py" }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
UserPromptSubmit writes the log as soon as a prompt arrives (so partial sessions are still captured if the agent crashes). Stop rewrites it at the end of the turn so the final assistant response is included.

Why prompt-and-response only
Signal density. A clean conversation log is readable later; a tool-call log is not.
PII / secret hygiene. Tool outputs often include env values, DB rows, API responses. Keeping them out of git history is a feature.
Reviewer ergonomics. The log answers "what did the human ask, what did the agent commit to?" — the diff answers "what did the agent do." Keeping them separate makes both more useful.
Repo size. Tool transcripts are huge. Prompt/response transcripts are kilobytes per session.
Per-project commit policy
In each repo's CLAUDE.md, add:

## Session Logs

Always commit `.claude-logs/` with your changes.
That's the entire enforcement mechanism. The log is generated automatically; the agent commits it as part of the normal "stage everything that changed" flow.

If a repo should not track logs (e.g. a public OSS project), add .claude-logs/ to its .gitignore instead.

Other agents
The script above is Claude Code-specific because it reads from ~/.claude/projects/<dashed-cwd>/*.jsonl. To port it:

Agent	Transcript location	Notes
Claude Code	~/.claude/projects/<dashed-cwd>/*.jsonl	Native — use the script as-is.
OpenAI Codex CLI	~/.codex/sessions/<id>.jsonl (varies by version)	Schema differs: messages have role + content directly; no isMeta/isSidechain. Adapt is_user_prompt and extract_assistant_text accordingly.
Cursor agent	No on-disk transcript by default	Out of scope — would need to hook the agent's request/response stream.
Aider	.aider.chat.history.md in cwd	Already markdown. Either commit it directly or strip tool calls with a small regex pass.
For any agent: the rule is read its own transcript, write only user-text + final-assistant-text to .claude-logs/<timestamp>_<session>.md, run on prompt-submit and stop. Keep the output filename pattern identical so a single repo can hold logs from multiple agents without collisions.

Troubleshooting
No log file appears. Check ~/.claude/projects/ exists and contains a directory matching your cwd with / replaced by - (leading dash kept). The script silently exits if the session dir doesn't exist.
Log is empty. Likely the session has only sidechain/agent-prefixed JSONL files. The script ignores agent-*.jsonl on purpose (subagents shouldn't pollute the main log).
Prompts contain pasted secrets. The script doesn't redact. If this is a concern, add a redaction pass in extract_clean_log before writing.
Multiple sessions in one repo on the same day. Each session gets its own file (named by session ID), so there's no overwrite risk.

1. Agent logging — the 8x guide, scoped to this repo
8x's guide is in `docs/agent-logging-setup.md` (save the guide there first). Read it in full before doing anything. Requirements:

* Use the `extract-log.py` script exactly as written in the guide. Do not change a byte of it.
* Output goes to `.claude-logs/<timestamp>_<session-id>.md` in the repo root, in the guide's format. `.claude-logs/` is committed, never gitignored.

One deliberate deviation, which I'll tell 8x about: the guide installs the hooks in `~/.claude/settings.json`, which would log every Claude Code session on this machine, including my work repos at Kpler. Instead:

* Save the script at `scripts/extract-log.py` in this repo, unchanged, executable.
* Wire the guide's two hooks (`UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop`, same commands) in the project's `.claude/settings.json`, committed, pointing at that script via `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`. Then Shakeb gets the same logging when he pulls.
* Check against the current Claude Code hooks docs, not memory, that project-level hooks fire for both events and that the script sees the right working directory.
* Don't touch `~/.claude/settings.json`. If a global hook from an earlier attempt exists, tell me rather than removing it.

Other agents. We'll also use Codex and Cursor. For each, look up the current docs, then follow the guide's "Other agents" section: same output directory, same filename pattern, same prompt-and-final-response-only content.

* Codex CLI: find its session transcript location and any notify or hook mechanism that runs automatically per turn. Adapt a copy of the script (e.g. `scripts/extract-log-codex.py`) to Codex's transcript format.
* Cursor: check whether Cursor now supports agent hooks that can capture the prompt and final response. If yes, wire it. If not, tell me plainly — I'll then use Cursor only as an editor, never its agent, on this repo. Unlogged AI work isn't acceptable: 8x's rule is "no logs, no rating".

Nothing may require me to remember to run it. If a mechanism isn't automatic, say so.
Verify, then prove it:

1. I'll send a canary in this session: `CAPTURE TEST — Playmakers C2, Nazmul`. Confirm the prompt and response both land in `.claude-logs/`, and that the frontmatter shows `author: Md Nazmul Ahsan`, `github_user: mnahsanofficial`, and my personal email.
2. I'll open a second session and send another canary; it must land in its own file.
3. Same for Codex, and Cursor if supported.
4. Write `docs/logging.md`: what's installed, the one deviation and why, per-agent status, and the canary results. Keep it short.

2. Agent instructions file
Create `AGENTS.md` as the single source of instructions for all three agents, and a `CLAUDE.md` that imports it. Include:

* Session logs: "Always commit `.claude-logs/` with your changes." (from the guide)
* Workflow: a branch per feature off `main`, a PR with a short description, merge commits only, never squash — squashing would collapse the code and log history that shows who did what, and we're rated individually.
* Pair rule: don't change files the other person is actively working on without saying so in the PR.
* Product principles from the brief: small daily sessions, practice not just content, visible progress, a reason to come back on day 3 and day 7. Mobile-first, checked at 390px. Test every flow by hand; don't trust that generated code works.
* Secrets: `.env.local` only, gitignored, never printed. These logs are public.

3. Minimal scaffold — local only
The brief says run and demo everything on localhost; no deployment needed.

* First check whether 8x has published their repo template. If it's available, tell me what's in it before we choose.
* Otherwise propose the fastest stack for two people to build a mobile-first app locally in nine days, in two or three lines, and wait for my OK. My default: Next.js App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind, and SQLite with Drizzle — zero database setup, one command to run.
* Scaffold only: the app runs with one command, one placeholder page, lint and typecheck passing, a `.env.example`, and a README with setup steps that work from a clean clone. No features.

4. Commit and report
Commit on a `chore/setup` branch: logging, `docs/logging.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, the scaffold and the logs so far. Open a PR and stop — Shakeb should see it before it merges.
Then report in five lines: logging status per agent, the stack, how to run it, anything I need to do, and anything that could silently stop logging later.

[.claude-logs/2026-10-28_16-18-53_04fdfec2….md](.claude-logs/2026-10-28_16-18-53_04fdfec2-4fbf-40b8-a62b-41b340716468.md) is a separate file holding that session's prompt (`CAPTURE TEST 2 — Playmakers C2, Nazmul`) and reply. Its frontmatter matches the first file: your name, `mnahsanofficial`, and your personal email.

The other session explained the cause correctly but got one point wrong. It said your messages could end up in the other session's file; they can't. Each log file is built only from its own session's transcript. I checked the first session's file: `CAPTURE TEST 2` appears there only inside my own earlier replies, where I suggested that text.

What can happen when two sessions run at once:
- The script isn't told which session triggered the hook, so it updates whichever session's transcript changed most recently. That can be the other session.
- When that happens, a session's newest turn is missing from its file until its next hook runs.
- If that session ends while the other one keeps going, its last turn stays missing until someone sends it another message.
- The safe habit is one Claude Code session per checkout at a time.

I've put this wording in [docs/logging.md](docs/logging.md), filled in canary row 2, and committed both log files on `docs/canary-results` (`4d79c7c`). It's still local, not pushed.

Pulling isn't quite enough. The hook config travels with the repo, but a few things on his machine decide whether his logs get written and whether they carry his name.

**Before he can pull**
1. **Access.** He isn't a collaborator on `mnahsanofficial/rhythm-health` yet. Add him with write access, and he accepts the invite.
2. **A fresh clone.** His current clone points at `shakeb03/Health`. He should clone `rhythm-health`, after you merge PR #2 so he gets the Codex fix.

**One-time setup on his machine**
3. **Git identity in the repo.** Logs record `git config user.name` and `user.email`, so he should run `git config user.name` and `git config user.email` inside the clone with his own details.
4. **GitHub CLI.** He needs `gh` installed and logged in, with his own account active. `github_user` comes from `gh api user`; without it his logs say `unknown`.
5. **`python3` on his PATH.** Without it the hook fails and nothing is logged.
6. **Mac or Linux.** The hook commands are shell commands; if he's on Windows they likely won't work as written. Tell me if he is.

**Per agent**
7. **Claude Code:** start the session at the repo root, not a subfolder. Accept the trust prompt for the folder, or the project hooks won't run.
8. **Codex:** trust the project and approve the hooks in `/hooks`. Until then Codex logs nothing and gives no warning, which is what happened to you.
9. **Cursor:** untested, so he shouldn't rely on it until a canary passes.

**Then prove it**
10. He sends one canary per agent he'll use. A new file should appear in `.claude-logs/` with his name, GitHub login and email in the frontmatter. If one doesn't, he should treat that agent as unlogged.

Same habit as you: one Claude Code session per checkout at a time.

If it helps, I can add this as a short "Teammate setup" section to `docs/logging.md` in a small PR, so he has it in the repo.

Playmakers C2 — basic screens and onboarding flow (paste into Claude Code)
Shakeb has pushed the PRD and TRD to the repo. My part is the UI: the basic screens and the onboarding flow. Shakeb is building the backend and APIs; I'll wire the UI to them once he says they're ready.
How we work (follow this exactly)

1. `git fetch` and create a new branch from the latest `origin/main`: `feat/ui-onboarding`. Show me `git log -1 origin/main` so I can see it's current.
2. You write the code. You do not commit, push or merge.
3. After each logical chunk, stop and give me:
   * the exact `git add` command for that chunk's files, always including `.claude-logs/` so the logs land next to the code they produced
   * a commit message: a short imperative subject line (under 70 characters), then one or two lines on why I'll commit it myself, then tell you to continue.
4. When everything is done, push the branch and open a PR with `gh`: what's in it, why, what's not in it, how to run it, and screenshots at 390px and 1440px. Don't merge. I'll merge it.

Keep the chunks small: one screen or one piece of the flow each.
Step 1 — read and plan. No code yet.
Read the PRD and TRD in full. Then give me, in chat:

* the list of screens for V1, and which ones this branch covers
* the onboarding steps and the data each one collects, matching the PRD
* the data shapes the screens will need, taken from the TRD's API design
* your design direction (see below): palette, type, the one memorable thing, and where motion is used
* anything in the PRD or TRD that's unclear or contradicts itself — ask me, don't guess

Wait for my OK before building.
Design direction
8x pointed us at their own products as the quality bar: https://www.karma.business/ and https://8xresearch.com/. Study them in a real browser, at 1440px and 390px, before proposing anything.
Take their qualities, not their look:

* how product UI is used as the illustration instead of stock imagery
* typography with a clear scale and real hierarchy
* calm, generous layout, and copy that's plain and specific
* motion that feels considered, not decorative

Don't copy their names, logos, palette or page layouts. 8x wants to see our own design choices. This is a health habits product, so the design should come from that subject: sleep, food, daily rhythm, small progress over time.
Motion: I want this to feel alive and delightful, so use more motion than a plain form — but every animation must answer something:

* onboarding steps transition smoothly, with a progress indicator that moves
* choices respond when tapped
* one memorable moment at the end of onboarding, where the user's profile "comes together" from their answers
* numbers and progress animate when they change Avoid a fade-up on every section. Respect `prefers-reduced-motion` everywhere. Use the motion library that fits our stack, and check its current docs.

Quality floor: mobile-first and checked at 390px, keyboard accessible with visible focus, good contrast, 16px inputs on phones, tap targets of at least 44px, skeletons shaped like the content, and copy in sentence case.
Food and health: no calorie counting, weight targets or medical claims anywhere. Ask about habits and routines ("Did you eat a vegetable at lunch?"), not numbers that can push people towards unhealthy eating.
Data before the APIs exist
The APIs aren't ready yet. So:

* define TypeScript types that match the TRD's API design exactly
* put sample data in one clearly named module (e.g. `src/lib/mock/`), never inline in components
* read it through a small data layer, so wiring the real API later is a swap in one place, not a rewrite
* say in the PR exactly which screens use sample data, so nobody mistakes it for real data

Check your work
Before handing me each chunk, run it in a real browser at 390px and 1440px, look at the screenshots, and fix what looks generic or broken: weak type, wasted space, placeholder copy, motion that stutters. Lint and typecheck must pass.
Start with Step 1.

time: 2026-10-28 21:42

The branch is ready, I've read both documents in full, and I've studied the two 8x sites at 1440px and 390px. No code yet. Nine questions at the end block the build.

```
a807a166dd99feced91d2f1edfe9be92b34a45fb
shakeb03  2026-10-28 16:28:18 -0400
docs: define Rhythm Health Lab product and technical architecture
```

`feat/ui-onboarding` starts from that commit. Shakeb pushed it straight to `main` instead of opening a PR, so the PR-only rule in `AGENTS.md` didn't hold; worth a quick word with him.

## Screens

The PRD lists nine V1 screens:

1. Persona onboarding
2. Home / experiment dashboard
3. Create experiment
4. AI experiment proposal
5. Prediction preview
6. Active experiment dashboard
7. Daily check-in
8. Experiment history
9. Interactive Lab Report

**This branch covers:**
- **Home for a new user:** "Stop guessing what works for you. Test it.", the "Run your first experiment" button, and the three example questions.
- **Persona onboarding:** every step plus the closing "your rhythm" moment.
- **A minimal app shell:** wordmark, page frame and focus styles that the other screens will reuse.

Screens 3–9 are later branches. See question 9 if "basic screens" meant empty pages for all nine.

## Onboarding steps

Each step fills one field of the TRD `profiles` table, which PRD §6 calls the persona.

| # | Step | Field and type | Input |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Why we ask (about a minute, nothing medical) | — | — |
| 1 | Age range | `age_range: string` | Single choice |
| 2 | Usual bedtime and wake time | `usual_sleep_schedule: { bedtime: "HH:MM", wake_time: "HH:MM" }` | Two time pickers |
| 3 | How much of that is sleep | `usual_sleep_hours: number` | Stepper in 0.5 steps, prefilled from step 2 |
| 4 | Activity level | `activity_level: string` | Single choice |
| 5 | Goals (1–3) | `goals: string[]` | Chips, plus "add your own" |
| 6 | Regular habits | `regular_habits: string[]` | Chips such as "Coffee around 9am", "Late dinner", "Phone in bed", plus your own |
| 7 | Constraints (optional) | `constraints: string[]` | Chips such as "Can't exercise before work", "Shift work", plus your own |
| 8 | Your rhythm (the profile comes together) | sends `PUT /api/profile` | — |

Food appears only as routines ("Late dinner", "Veg at lunch"). No calories, weight or medical questions anywhere.

## Data shapes from the TRD

These would go in `src/lib/types/api.ts`, read through `src/lib/data/` and backed by sample data in `src/lib/mock/`:

```ts
type Profile = {
  id: string;
  age_range: AgeRange;
  usual_sleep_hours: number;
  usual_sleep_schedule: { bedtime: string; wake_time: string }; // "HH:MM"
  activity_level: ActivityLevel;
  goals: string[];
  regular_habits: string[];
  constraints: string[];
  created_at: string;
  updated_at: string;
};
type ProfileInput = Omit<Profile, "id" | "created_at" | "updated_at">; // PUT /api/profile, no user_id
```

The data layer would expose `getProfile()`, `saveProfile(input)` and `getActiveExperiment()`. The last one is what tells home whether you're a new or returning user. I'll add the experiment, prediction, check-in and report types in the branches that use them, so this branch doesn't carry types it never exercises.

## Design direction

**What I'm taking from 8x, without copying the look:**
- Karma puts its own product console on the page as the illustration, labelled "Shown with example data". That's my model for using real product UI instead of stock images.
- 8x Research uses a two-tone headline: a dark line, then a quieter second line.
- 8x Research uses numbered steps such as `01 / SPOT`.
- Both write short, specific copy.
- Their motion follows the story rather than fading in every section.

**Palette, taken from a day's light** (deliberately not Karma's orange/violet or 8x's dark-with-a-gradient-orb):

| Role | Name | Colour |
|---|---|---|
| Background | Oat | `#F6F3EC` |
| Text | Ink | `#1B2421` |
| Actions, selected states | Moss | `#2F6B4F` |
| Sleep | Night | `#263A6B` |
| Daylight markers (never text) | Sun | `#F2C14E` |

Light mode only for now. I'll check every text pair against WCAG AA.

**Type:**
- Fraunces for display: a soft, warm serif, and neither 8x site uses a serif.
- Instrument Sans for interface text.
- Geist Mono with tabular figures for times and numbers.
- Scale: 14 / 16 / 18 / 24 / 32 / 44 / 60.
- Inputs are 16px on phones, and all copy is in sentence case.

**The one memorable thing: the rhythm ring.** A 24-hour dial that your answers draw onto.
- Your bedtime-to-wake time becomes a night arc.
- Habits become small markers at their times of day.
- It builds live during onboarding.
- At the end it assembles into your profile.
- Later, the dashboard and Lab Report reuse it as the product's illustration.

**Where motion goes, and why:**
- **Moving between steps:** the content slides in the direction you're going. A segmented progress bar fills with a spring.
- **Tapping a choice:** it presses down slightly, a tick draws in, and in single-choice lists the highlight glides to your new pick.
- **Answering a sleep step:** the ring's night arc sweeps to the new times, and the hours figure counts to its new value.
- **Final moment:** the arc draws, the habit markers settle in one by one, then your summary lines appear. This happens once and isn't decoration.
- **Reduced motion:** with `prefers-reduced-motion` on, everything above becomes an instant change or a simple opacity fade.

**Library:** `motion` 14.7, imported from `motion/react` (React 19 is supported). `MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"` sits at the root, and `useReducedMotion` handles the ring. Motion's `AnimateNumber` is a paid add-on, so number counting uses the free `animate()` function.

**Quality floor:**
- Tap targets of at least 44px.
- Visible focus rings.
- Full keyboard flow: arrow keys within a choice group, Enter to continue.
- Loading skeletons shaped like the ring and the chips.
- Checked at 390px and 1440px.

## Questions before I build

1. **Folder layout.** The TRD puts the app in `frontend/` with FastAPI in `backend/`. Ours sits at the repo root in `src/`. Do I build in `src/` as it is, or move to `frontend/` first? That's easier now than later.
2. **Database.** The TRD uses Supabase Postgres behind FastAPI, so our SQLite/Drizzle setup is never used. That's Shakeb's area: should it stay, or should I leave it alone and he removes it?
3. **Sign-in timing.** `PUT /api/profile` needs a signed-in user. Does onboarding happen before sign-up, with the answers held in the browser and saved after "Create your account"? Or does sign-in come first? Is the sign-in screen part of this branch?
4. **Experiment length.** The PRD says 7 days ("Day 4 / 7", a 7-day timeline). The TRD says 3 baseline days plus 7 intervention days, so 10. Which one does the UI show, and does home say "7-day"?
5. **Allowed values.** The TRD only gives examples. I propose:
   - `age_range`: `18-24`, `25-34`, `35-44`, `45-54`, `55-64`, `65+`
   - `activity_level`: `mostly_sedentary`, `lightly_active`, `moderately_active`, `very_active`

   Can Shakeb confirm, or send his lists?
6. **Habit times.** `regular_habits` is plain text, so the ring can't know when a habit happens. Can it be `{ label, time? }`? If not, I'll attach times only to the preset chips, inside the UI.
7. **Sleep hours vs schedule.** If the two disagree, which wins? I'll prefill hours from the schedule minus 30 minutes and let the user adjust.
8. **No profile yet.** Does `GET /api/profile` return 404 or `null` before a profile exists? What does an error response look like? The new-vs-returning home logic needs this.
9. **"Basic screens".** Does it mean only home and onboarding as above, or also empty, navigable pages for all nine screens?

I'll start chunk 1 (types, sample data, data layer and app shell) once you've answered or told me to use my defaults.

**What's in chunk 4**
- **Hero:**
  - The PRD's headline "Stop guessing what works for you. Test it.", with "Test it." in a quieter grey on a second line.

**What the browser check caught and I fixed**
- **The night arc never drew.** Watching each SVG path for scrolling into view didn't fire, so the ring as a whole now triggers it.
- **Hydration mismatch.** The server and the browser computed `Math.sin` slightly differently. Coordinates are now rounded, and the served SVG has no long decimals.
- **Smaller fixes:**
  - The "06" and "18" labels collided with tick marks.
  - The mono "7.5" looked heavy; it's in the display serif now.
  - The example questions looked like tappable buttons.
  - The button text wrapped onto two lines at 1440px.

Chunk 5 is done: the onboarding frame, the intro and the age step. I tested it by hand at 390px and 1440px, and lint and typecheck pass.

**What's in it**
- **`/onboarding` ([onboarding-flow.tsx](frontend/src/components/onboarding/onboarding-flow.tsx)):**
  - An intro screen: three things we'll ask, plus "Nothing medical, and no weights or calories. Your answers stay on this device until you create an account at the end."
  - Then the questions, one at a time.
- **Moving between steps:** the content slides the way you're going (forward to the left, Back to the right) and fades out quickly on exit. With reduced motion on, Motion's setting drops the slide and keeps only the fade.
- **Progress ([progress-bar.tsx](frontend/src/components/onboarding/progress-bar.tsx)):**
  - Seven segments, with the current one filling on a spring, plus "01 / 07".
  - It counts all seven planned questions from the start, so it's accurate from the first one.
  - Screen readers announce "Question 1 of 7".
- **Choices ([choice-group.tsx](frontend/src/components/onboarding/choice-group.tsx)):**
  - Real radio buttons sit under cards with 56px tap targets.
  - The green highlight glides to whatever you pick, and a tick draws in.
  - Each card presses down slightly when tapped.
- **Saving ([draft.ts](frontend/src/lib/onboarding/draft.ts)):** answers and your current step are kept in this browser, so a reload picks up where you left off. The server sends a loading placeholder shaped like a question step.
- **Buttons:** Back and Continue stay pinned to the bottom of the screen on phones. On desktop they sit just under the question.
- **Continue:** disabled until you've answered. Enter submits the step.

**What testing by hand caught and fixed**

## Fix for the failed commit

That error was my fault: the message I gave you put `"your rhythm"` inside a double-quoted `-m`, so the shell split it apart. Git aborted the commit, and chunk 9 is still staged. This version passes the message through a heredoc, so the quotes are safe:

```bash
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
feat(frontend): add the "your rhythm" closing moment

The ring assembles from the user's answers, then a summary they can
edit and save. Saving goes through the data layer, so the real API
slots in later without touching this screen.
EOF
```

The session log has changed again since you staged it, so run `git add .claude-logs/` first.

## Where things stand

- **Branch:** `feat/ui-onboarding`, 7 commits ahead of its remote copy (chunks 2–8), plus chunk 9 staged.
- **New on `origin/main`:** 7 commits from Shakeb, merged through PR #4.
  - `e833a71` scaffold Next.js frontend and FastAPI backend
  - `34b4938` Supabase email/password auth, plus a protected endpoint
  - `a6c20e3` profiles table and `GET`/`PUT /api/profile`

- **Logging is untouched:** none of his commits change `.claude/settings.json` or `scripts/`, and his session logs are included.

## The main conflict: where the frontend lives

We both moved the app into `frontend/`. He put it in `frontend/app/`, `frontend/components/` and `frontend/lib/`, matching the TRD. I put it in `frontend/src/…`. If both folders exist, Next.js uses `app/` and silently ignores `src/app/`, so my screens would disappear without any error.

My recommendation: before merging, move my code into his layout as its own commit on this branch.
- `frontend/src/app` → `frontend/app`, and the same for `components` and `lib`.
- Use the same `@/*` → `./*` import path he uses.
- Delete the old SQLite and Drizzle files (`frontend/src/db`, `/api/health`, `drizzle.config.ts`), which he also removed.

The merge commit then only joins the two lines of work. Doing the move inside the merge would also work, but it would hide a large restructure inside a merge commit, which is harder to review and attribute.

## Each conflict and my proposed resolution

These come from a dry-run merge that only reads Git's history and doesn't touch your files.

| 8 | `frontend/app/page.tsx` | both added | My home page. His `AuthPanel` and `BackendStatus` stay as components, and I'll reuse the auth panel when wiring sign-in in step 4. |
| 9 | `frontend/app/favicon.ico` | moved to two places | One copy at `frontend/app/favicon.ico`. |
| 10 | `frontend/package.json` | both edited | Combine: his name and `@supabase/supabase-js`, plus my `motion`. Drop `drizzle-orm`, `better-sqlite3`, `drizzle-kit` and the `db:*` scripts. |

Step 2 is done except for one thing: the env files aren't on disk yet, and the backend expects a different filename than the one you used. I printed no values at any point.

## Env files

- **Neither file exists in this checkout.** There's no `frontend/.env.local`, `backend/.env` or `backend/.env.local`. You may have created them in another folder, or not saved them yet.
- **The backend's filename is `.env.local`, not `.env`.** `backend/app/config.py` reads `env_file=".env.local"`, so a `backend/.env` would be silently ignored and every key would be empty.

To create both from the examples, run these from the repo root, then paste your values in your editor (not in this chat):

```bash
cp frontend/.env.example frontend/.env.local
```

```bash
cp backend/.env.example backend/.env.local
```

If you already have a filled-in `backend/.env` somewhere, move it to `backend/.env.local` instead of copying the example.

## Checks

- **Ignored by Git:** `frontend/.env.local`, `backend/.env` and `backend/.env.local` all match the root `.gitignore` rule `.env*`.
- **No real keys anywhere.**
  - I scanned the working tree and all 31 commits for OpenRouter, Anthropic and Supabase keys, JWTs and private keys, printing file names only. Nothing matched.
  - No `.env` file other than `.env.example` has ever been committed.
- **The service-role key is backend-only.** It's read only in `backend/app/config.py` and `backend/app/integrations/supabase.py`. The frontend uses only `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, and Next.js only loads env files from `frontend/`.
- **Trailing slashes:**
  - Backend, with `SUPABASE_URL` ending in `/`: safe. Every join calls `.rstrip('/')` first, including the default JWT issuer.
  - Frontend, with `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` set to `http://localhost:8001/`: broken. Shakeb's `frontend/lib/api/client.ts` builds `http://localhost:8001//health`, and I confirmed FastAPI answers `//health` with 404 while `/health` returns 200. I'll fix the join in the frontend API client when wiring, and note it in the PR, since it's his file.
  - The Supabase JS client handles a trailing slash itself.
- **`OPENROUTER_REASONING_MODEL` empty:** a validator in `config.py` turns an empty value into the default, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`, the TRD's recommended model. An empty `OPENROUTER_JEV_MODEL` likewise becomes `typesafe/jev-router`. So you don't need a value from Shakeb unless you want a different model.
- **OpenRouter calls so far: 0.**

## Running

- **Backend:** running on :8001 via `uv`. Its dependencies are installed, and `uv run pytest` gives 97 passed. `/health` returns `ok`. `/health/supabase` returns `unavailable`, which is expected with no keys yet.
- **Frontend:** running on :3000.
- **Launch config:** I made Shakeb's backend entry in `.claude/launch.json` use a relative path during the merge, and the backend now starts from it.

## Logging is unchanged

- `extract-log.py` still has the same SHA-256 (`440fc039…a735`).
- This session's log was rewritten when you sent your last message, so the hook still fires. That message will appear in the log after this reply, as usual.

Both apps now run with your keys, and I've read Shakeb's whole API: every route, schema, service and migration. One blocker before step 3 can finish: I can't call the endpoints that need a login yet, because there's no sign-in screen.

**Env and running**
- Every variable is set, except the two that are meant to be empty.
- The frontend has the `anon` key and the backend has the `service_role` key, both for the same Supabase project. I checked each key's role claim without printing the keys.
- Both `.env.local` files are ignored by Git.
- `CORS_ORIGINS` isn't set, so it uses the default `http://localhost:3000`. I confirmed the browser's CORS check passes.
- `/health/supabase` returns `ok`, and `/api/profile` and `/api/me` return 401 without a token.
- **OpenRouter calls so far: 0.**

## Contract

All `/api/*` routes need `Authorization: Bearer <Supabase access token>`. No route accepts a user ID or profile ID; the backend gets both from the token.

| Endpoint | Request | Response | Errors | My screen |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Supabase `signUp` / `signInWithPassword` (from the browser) | email, password | session | Supabase's message | Sign in / sign up (to build) |
| `GET /api/profile` | — | `Profile` | **404 when no profile exists yet**, 503 if the database is down | Home (new vs returning) |
| `PUT /api/profile` | the profile fields, no extra fields allowed | `Profile` | 422 if validation fails, 503 | End of onboarding |
| `POST /api/experiments/propose` | `{question}`, 5–500 characters | `{proposal, prediction, safety, classification}`; when blocked, `proposal` and `prediction` are `null` and `safety` explains why | 409 no profile, 503 AI down, 502 AI returned bad output | Ask a question (to build) |
| `POST /api/experiments` | `{proposal, prediction, category}`, which is what propose returned | `Experiment`, status `proposed` (201) | 422 if the safety check fails again, 409 no profile | Accept the proposal (to build) |
| `POST /api/experiments/{id}/start` | — | `Experiment`, status `active` | 409 if not in `proposed` state or one is already active, 404 | Start (to build) |
| `GET /api/experiments?status=active` | — | list of `Experiment` | 409 no profile | Home (returning user) |
| `GET /api/experiments/{id}` | — | `Experiment` with computed `progress` | 404 | Active experiment (to build) |

## Where the code differs from the TRD

1. **Each proposal makes 2–4 paid model calls.** Shakeb merged design and prediction into one call, but the Jev classification is also a call through OpenRouter, and an invalid answer triggers one retry. So expect about 2 calls per proposal in normal use.
2. **Jev runs through OpenRouter** (`typesafe/jev-router`), not through the separate `JEV_API_KEY` and `JEV_API_URL` that TRD §38 lists.
3. **Propose also returns `classification`**, which the save step needs for `category`.
4. **Experiments include a computed `progress` object** (`state`, `day_number`, `phase`, `phase_day`, `days_remaining`, `ends_at`). It isn't in the TRD, but it's exactly what the dashboard needs.
5. **There's no endpoint to regenerate only the prediction.** The TRD says the prediction "may be regenerated" before the experiment starts; here you'd re-run propose, which costs 2+ calls.
6. **Safety can return `allowed: true` with a caution** (`risk: review` gives "Keep this experiment gentle…"). The UI needs to show it.
7. **Check-ins, completion and the report** don't exist yet, as you said.

## Mismatches with my UI (all fixed on my side, no backend changes)

1. **Activity level:** the backend expects `sedentary`, not my `mostly_sedentary`.
2. **No profile yet:** `GET /api/profile` returns 404, not `null` as my sample data assumed.
3. **List limits:** the backend allows at most 20 items per list and 200 characters each. My habits step has no limit yet, so I'll cap it.
4. **Trailing slash:** `apiGet` produces `//` URLs, which return 404. I'll fix the join in the shared frontend API client and flag it in the PR, since it's Shakeb's file.

## How I'd build the wiring

The proposal, prediction, start and active-experiment screens don't exist in my branch yet, so wiring them means building them. One commit per chunk, as before:

- **A. Foundations:** fix the API client (slash join, typed errors, auth header) and replace my guessed types with the real backend shapes.
- **B. Sign in / sign up**, using his Supabase client in my design, with the account step at the end of onboarding.
- **C. Onboarding saves through `PUT /api/profile`**, and home checks `GET /api/profile`.
- **D. Ask a question:** propose, with loading, blocked and caution states.
- **E. Proposal and prediction screen**, then save and start.
- **F. A minimal active-experiment screen** using `progress`.

**On testing:** creating an account goes through Supabase's hosted sign-up, so I can't make the test user myself. After chunk B, please sign up in the in-app browser. I'll then test each endpoint through the page's own session, never copying the token anywhere.

**On cost:** I'll aim for a single proposal to prove the flow: one request, about 2 paid model calls. I'll tell you exactly how many I make.

````markdown
# Rhythm Health Lab: finish the UI wiring (handoff from Claude Code)

Read `PRD.md` and `TRD.md` before writing code.

## 0. Logging first. Stop if it fails.

8x rates us only on logged agent work ("no logs, no rating"). All logs go in `.claude-logs/`, one folder for every agent.

1. Confirm `.codex/hooks.json` exists and runs `scripts/extract-log-codex.py` on `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop`. Don't edit either file. Editing the hooks file removes the hook approval.
2. Ask me to make sure the project is trusted and the hooks are approved (`/hooks` in the Codex CLI).
3. I'll send a canary: `CAPTURE TEST — Codex, Nazmul`. After your reply, check that a new file in `.claude-logs/` contains my prompt, your reply, and frontmatter with `agent: codex`, `author: Md Nazmul Ahsan`, `github_user: mnahsanofficial` and `git_email: mnahsanofficial@gmail.com`.
4. If there's no file, stop and tell me. Don't do any other work until logging works.

## How we work (follow exactly)

- You write code. You never commit, push or merge. Never rebase or force-push.
- Work in small chunks, one screen or one piece of a flow each. After each chunk, stop and give me:
  - the exact `git add` command for that chunk's files, always including `.claude-logs/`, never any env file;
  - a commit message in a heredoc, because quotes in `-m` broke a commit before:
    ```bash
    git commit -F - <<'EOF'
    feat(frontend): short imperative subject under 70 chars

    One or two lines on why.
    EOF
    ```
  - then wait for "committed, continue".
- Before each handover, run it in a real browser at 390px and 1440px, look at it, and fix anything broken or generic. `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` must pass, from `frontend/`.
- If the frontend needs something the backend doesn't provide, don't edit any file under `backend/` or `supabase/`. Add it to the "Requests for Shakeb" list below.

## Secrets and cost (hard rules)

- `frontend/.env.local` and `backend/.env.local` hold real keys. Never print, echo, cat or log them, and never write them anywhere else. Check variables by name only ("set / not set").
- The service-role key is backend-only. The frontend may use only `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`.
- `POST /api/experiments/propose` costs real OpenRouter money: one Jev classification call plus one design-and-prediction call, plus one retry if the output is invalid. Propose as few times as possible. Count every call and report the count. So far: 0.
- Never create accounts or type passwords into Supabase yourself. I sign in in the browser. To test an API directly, use the page's own session (`sb-*-auth-token` in localStorage) inside page JS, and never output the token.

## Running locally

- Backend: `cd backend && uv run uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8001`. Tests: `uv run pytest`, 97 pass.
- Frontend: `cd frontend && npm run dev`, at http://localhost:3000.
- Known dev-only quirk: edits to `frontend/app/globals.css` sometimes don't reach the browser until you restart `npm run dev`. Check the served CSS before assuming your CSS is wrong.

## What already exists (don't redo)

- `frontend/` uses `app/`, `components/` and `lib/`, with `@/*` pointing at `./*`.
- `lib/api/client.ts`: `apiGet(path, auth)`, `apiSend(method, path, body, auth=true)`, and `ApiError(status, message)`. Status 0 means the server was unreachable. It already trims a trailing slash from the API URL; without that, `//path` returns 404.
- `lib/types/api.ts` mirrors `backend/app/schemas/*` exactly. Use these types.
- `lib/data/profile.ts`: `getProfile()` returns null on 404; `saveProfile()`. Screens get data only through `lib/data/*`, never straight from the client or from mock data.
- `lib/mock/profile.ts`: sample data, used only for the home illustration. Sample data for features the backend lacks (check-ins, completion, analysis, lab report) goes only in `lib/mock/`, and every screen using it must be labelled.
- Auth: Shakeb's `lib/auth/supabase.ts` and `lib/auth/session.tsx` (`useSession()`). `components/auth/auth-form.tsx` handles sign-in and sign-up, `/sign-in?next=` exists, and `components/auth-nav.tsx` is in the header.
- Onboarding (`/onboarding`): seven steps, then a summary that creates the account if needed and saves the profile via `PUT /api/profile`. Returning users start from their saved answers.
- Home (`components/home-cta.tsx`): with a profile, it shows "Test a new question", linking to `/experiments/new`, which doesn't exist yet.
- Design:
  - Tokens in `app/globals.css`: oat, paper, ink, muted, line, moss, moss-tint, night, sun (never used for text), clay and clay-tint for errors.
  - Fonts: Fraunces for display, Instrument Sans for text, Geist Mono for numbers.
  - Components: `ChoiceGroup`, `ChipPicker`, `RhythmRing`, `ProgressBar`.
- Motion uses `motion/react` (14.7).
  - Every animation must have a reason; no fade-up on every section.
  - Reduced motion is respected: `MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"` at the root, plus `useReducedMotion` for SVG.
  - Don't put `whileTap` on labels or wrappers of native inputs. It makes them focusable and breaks keyboard use. Use CSS `active:` instead.
  - Round SVG coordinates (see `lib/rhythm/time.ts`), or server and browser render differently.
  - Forms can't nest: the onboarding summary sits outside the step form.
- Quality floor: mobile-first, checked at 390px; keyboard accessible with visible focus; 16px+ inputs; 44px+ tap targets; skeletons shaped like the content; sentence case; every API call has loading, error and empty states, and no silent failures (`.catch(() => {})` isn't allowed). No calorie counting, weight targets or medical claims anywhere.

## Backend contract (read from the code; check it again before wiring)

Every `/api/*` route needs `Authorization: Bearer <Supabase access token>`. No route accepts a user or profile ID.

- `POST /api/experiments/propose`, body `{question}` (5–500 characters). Returns `{proposal, prediction, safety, classification}`.
  - When blocked: `proposal` and `prediction` are null, `safety.allowed` is false, and `reason` and `suggested_alternative` are set.
  - With `classification.risk === "review"`: allowed, with `safety.reason` as a caution to show.
  - Errors: 409 "Create your profile first", 503 AI unavailable, 502 invalid AI output, 422 validation.
- `POST /api/experiments`, body `{proposal, prediction, category}`, all three from propose. Returns `Experiment` with status `proposed` (201). Errors: 422 if the safety re-check fails, 409 no profile.
- `POST /api/experiments/{id}/start` returns `Experiment` with status `active`. Errors: 409 if not proposed or one is already active, 404.
- `GET /api/experiments?status=active|proposed|completed` returns a list. `GET /api/experiments/{id}` returns `Experiment`, including a computed `progress` object: `state`, `day_number`, `phase`, `phase_day`, `phase_days`, `total_days`, `days_remaining`, `started_at`, `ends_at`.
- Not built yet: check-ins, complete, report, and any way to delete or abandon an experiment. So once your test user starts an experiment, it stays active.

## Remaining chunks

- **D. `/experiments/new` ("Ask a question")**
  - A question field (16px+, 5–500 characters) and starter suggestions from the profile's goals plus the PRD §20 examples.
  - Guard pages: signed out goes to `/sign-in?next=/experiments/new`; no profile goes to `/onboarding`.
  - Propose with a real loading state, since it can take several seconds: a skeleton shaped like the proposal, plus calm copy about what's happening.
  - The states listed above:
    - blocked: show the reason, with "Use this instead" filling in `suggested_alternative`;
    - review: a caution banner;
    - 409, 503, 502 (with Retry) and 422 errors.
  - Add `lib/data/experiments.ts` for all experiment calls.
- **E. Proposal and prediction preview** (same route, the next state):
  - Show the question, hypothesis, intervention (action, timing, frequency), the timeline (3 baseline days, then 7 test days, from the proposal's numbers) and the metrics.
  - Show the prediction: the expected change range as a visual range bar, confidence, reasoning and uncertainty factors. Animate the numbers into place, and keep it honest: ranges, not false precision.
  - Actions:
    - "Start this experiment": `POST /api/experiments`, then `POST /{id}/start`, then go to `/experiments/{id}`. Handle a 409 "already active" by linking to the active one.
    - "Ask something else".
  - The prediction becomes fixed once started, so say so before they start.
- **F. `/experiments/[id]`** (minimal active-experiment screen):
  - The question; "Day X of N", from `progress`; the phase (baseline or test); days remaining; a timeline of every day (today highlighted, future days empty); the prediction card; the intervention.
  - Check-ins don't exist yet. Say so plainly ("Daily check-ins arrive soon") rather than faking data.
  - Also update home: with an active experiment, show it ("Day 2 of 10") and link to it.
- **G. Full test by hand at 390px and 1440px:**
  - I sign in. You walk the flow: onboarding, profile saved, propose (once, roughly 2 paid calls), the prediction, start.
  - Reload and confirm through the API that the experiment is `active` and the profile is saved.
  - Save screenshots of each screen at both widths to `docs/screenshots/ui-onboarding/` (390px and 1440px), and include them in a chunk commit.
- **H. PR:**
  - Ask me before pushing, then open the PR with `gh pr create -R mnahsanofficial/rhythm-health --base main`. Don't merge.
  - Body:
    - what's in it and why, and what's not in it;
    - how to run it;
    - screenshots, linking the committed images;
    - what's wired to the real API and what still uses sample data or isn't built;
    - the "Requests for Shakeb" list;
    - end with a line saying which parts Claude Code built and which parts Codex built.

## Requests for Shakeb (keep this list; add to it)

1. There's no endpoint to complete, abandon or delete an experiment, so a test account gets stuck with one active experiment.
2. There's no way to regenerate only the prediction (the TRD says it "may be regenerated" before start); re-proposing costs 2 or more paid calls.
3. TRD §38 lists `JEV_API_KEY` and `JEV_API_URL`, but the code runs Jev through OpenRouter (`OPENROUTER_JEV_MODEL`). Update the TRD.

7. The PRD/TRD commit went straight to `main`, but AGENTS.md says to use PRs. Please use PRs.

Before building anything, one timing problem: your real experiment can't run to its natural end before demo day. It began on 28 Oct at 23:29 UTC and runs 10 rolling 24-hour days, so it ends on **7 Nov**, but the hackathon ends on 6 Nov. The plan below needs your OK, and four questions need answers. No code is written yet.

`feat/ui-checkins` is created from the latest `main` (`0f9199d`). I've read Shakeb's new routes, schemas, analysis and migrations, and checked your account read-only.

**Your live experiment (`8a89e4fb`)**
- 3 baseline days, then 7 test days. It's on day 1 (baseline), with no check-ins and no report yet.
- Its metrics:
  - `fall_asleep_ease`: scale 1–10, the primary metric
  - `sleep_quality`: scale 1–10
  - `morning_focus`: scale 1–10
  - `minutes_to_fall_asleep`: a plain number

**What this means for demo day.** The backend lets you finish early once you have at least 2 baseline check-ins and 3 test-day check-ins. A check-in only ever counts for the current day; missed days can't be filled in later. So the earliest you could finish is around 3 Nov, and only if you check in every day.

## Contract (read from the code)

| Endpoint | Request | Response | Errors |
|---|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/experiments/{id}/checkins` | `metric_values`: one value per metric, all required, no extras. Scales are whole numbers within the metric's min and max; durations 0–1440 minutes; plain numbers ±1,000,000. `intervention_completed` is required on test days and must be false or left out on baseline days. Optional `note` up to 500 characters. | The check-in for **today only**; sending again replaces it. | 409: experiment completed, not started, or its days are over. **422 as a list of `{field, message}`.** 404, 503. |
| `GET /api/experiments/{id}/checkins` | — | All check-ins, oldest day first | 404 |
| `POST /api/experiments/{id}/complete` | — | `{experiment, report}` | 409: already completed or not active. **409 with an object** containing the counts you have and need, when there aren't enough check-ins. |
| `GET /api/experiments/{id}/report` | — | `{experiment_id, analysis, interpretation, learnings, next_experiment, created_at}` | 404 "No report for this experiment yet" |

**The analysis includes:**
- Adherence: days logged and done, adherence and coverage rates, missing days.
- Per metric: baseline and test-phase mean, median and spread; the absolute and percentage change; effect size; how consistently it moved in one direction; outlier days.
- Prediction against result: whether the result fell in the predicted range, whether the direction was right, and an `accuracy_percent` from 0 to 100.
- A signal: one of the four PRD categories, with reason codes such as `consistent_direction` and `low_adherence`.

**Where the code differs from the TRD and PRD**
1. **The report has only the calculated analysis.** `interpretation`, `learnings` and `next_experiment` are always empty for now, because the AI writing step isn't built. So PRD §15's "What we learned about you" and "What to test next" can't be real yet.
2. **There's no chart data in the report** (TRD §26). The chart will be built from the check-ins list instead, which is fine.
3. **Early finishing is allowed** after 2 baseline and 3 test check-ins.
4. **The demo seed is missing:** the seeded demo endpoint (TRD §28, `/api/demo/reset`) isn't built. Without it, we can't show a Lab Report with real data until 3 Nov at the earliest.
5. **Two new error shapes**, shown in bold in the table. Our API client only understands the old ones, so it would show "Request failed (422)".
6. **Days roll over at your start time, not at midnight.** A day runs 24 hours from 23:29 UTC, which is 05:29 in Dhaka, so a check-in at 5 am still counts toward the day before.

## Plan: one commit per chunk, as before

- **A. Foundations.**
  - Teach the API client the two new error shapes, including per-field messages for the check-in form.
  - Add types for check-ins, the analysis, the report and the complete response.
  - Add `lib/data/checkins.ts` and `lib/data/reports.ts`.
- **B. Today's check-in** at `/experiments/[id]/check-in`. This is the PRD's top mobile priority, aiming for under 30 seconds.
  - **Fields** come from each experiment's own metrics:
    - scales as a 1–10 tap grid of 44px buttons in two rows of five;
    - plain numbers with a numeric keypad;
    - durations in minutes;
    - yes/no for true-or-false metrics.
  - **Test days add one question:** "Did you do it today?", showing the intervention's action. Baseline days say "just record a normal day".
  - **Editing:** if today's check-in exists, the form opens prefilled.
  - **States:** errors appear next to the matching field, then "Day 2 recorded" with the timeline updating.
- **C. The experiment page.**
  - The timeline uses real check-ins; tapping a logged day shows its values and note, as PRD §13 asks.
  - It shows adherence, "Check in for day X" or "Done for today", and "Finish and see results" once the minimum is reached, warning that finishing early means less evidence.
  - A 409 for too few check-ins shows the counts.
  - I'll also fix the Next.js warning Codex saw on this page.
- **D. Lab Report** at `/experiments/[id]/report`, following PRD §15 with only what's real:
  - the question;
  - predicted against observed, with a range bar and a marker for the actual result;
  - a tappable chart of the primary metric each day: baseline against test days, with the predicted range shaded, and skipped days shown;
  - signal or noise, with the reason codes in plain words;
  - prediction accuracy, with the PRD's note that it isn't scientific confidence.

  "What we learned" and "What to test next" will say they're coming, rather than showing invented text.
- **E. Home:** the active-experiment button changes to "Check in for day X" when a check-in is due.
- **F.** Test everything by hand at 390px and 1440px, then open a PR.

**No paid AI calls:** none of these endpoints call OpenRouter.

## Questions (my recommendation first)

1. **Early finish:** offer "Finish and see results" as soon as the minimum is reached, with a warning, because the 7 Nov end date is after the demo. Or only after the last day?
2. **Report data before 3 Nov:** I build the report against the real endpoint and test it with mocked responses (as with E), and we ask Shakeb for the TRD §28 demo seed, so the demo has a real completed experiment to show.
3. **Your real check-ins:** I test the form with mocks, and you enter your own real values each day. I won't invent health data for your account. Today's check-in is the first test.
4. **Check-in placement:** its own page, `/experiments/[id]/check-in`, for focus and a short daily visit, linked from home and the experiment page.

## To add to the requests for Shakeb

- The TRD §28 demo seed endpoint, which the Lab Report demo needs.
- Day boundaries: decide whether days should follow the user's local morning rather than the start time plus 24 hours.
- Real experiments end after demo day, so the seed matters even more.

Chunk D, the Lab Report, is done. I tested it at 390px and 1440px against your real experiment, which correctly shows "No results yet.", and against a full mocked report. Your account is untouched: still active, with **0 check-ins**. Lint and typecheck pass, and no paid AI calls were made.

**How I tested a report that can't exist yet.** I didn't hand-write fake numbers. I ran Shakeb's actual `analyze()` function on your experiment's real definition plus 9 mocked days: day 8 missed, and day 7 with the change skipped and a note. That produced a report exactly as his backend would. It's also an awkward case for the page: a likely signal of **+32%**, far above the predicted 2–10%, so **0% prediction accuracy**.

**What's on the page** (`/experiments/[id]/report`), following PRD §15:
1. **The question**, with "Lab Report · finished 7 November".
2. **Signal or noise**, which comes first because it's the answer:
   - the classification as a tag, here "Likely signal", with a plain summary;
   - the main measure's change, here "+32% … (4.7 → 6.2 on average)";
   - each reason code in plain words, for example "Most test days moved in the same direction";
   - "A short personal experiment shows a pattern, not proof."
3. **Prediction vs reality:** "We predicted +2% to +10%" against "You saw +32%", counting up from zero. The range bar draws in, then a "You" marker drops into place. Accuracy shows as 0%, with the PRD's note that it isn't scientific confidence. The screen-reader label says the result was "outside the range".
4. **How the days went:** days logged per phase, how often you made the change, and missed days ("Day 8").
5. **Day by day:** each day's value for the main measure, with test days shaded and the predicted range drawn as a band in the measure's own units. The baseline average is a dashed line, days where you skipped the change are hollow dots, and a missed day leaves a gap in the line. Every dot is a 44px button with a screen-reader label, and tapping one shows that day's values and note.
6. **The other things you tracked:** baseline average to test average, plus the percentage change.
7. **What we learned, and what to test next:** the backend leaves these empty for now, so the page says they're "on the way", with a "Test a new question" button. I didn't invent takeaways.

**Other states:** a placeholder shaped like the report while loading, "No results yet" before the experiment is finished, not found, a load error with Retry, and signed out taking you to sign-in and back. The page reads its URL parameter inside Suspense, like the others.

**Fixed during testing**
- **Chart lines:** they were partly missing, because drawing them in with `pathLength` breaks when the chart stretches. They now fade in instead.
- **Baseline average line:** it looked solid on desktop because the dash spacing stretched with the chart. It's now an HTML dashed rule, consistent at every width.
- **Mobile wrapping:** "5 of 6 test days" wrapped badly, so it's now "5 of 6" under "Made the change, test days". Missed days read "Day 8".

Documentation only. No changes to application code, config, dependencies or
tests in this task.

Workflow, same as always:
- git fetch and create a branch from the latest origin/main: docs/status-and-readme.
  Show me `git log -1 origin/main` first.
- You write the docs. You never commit, push or merge.
- After each chunk, give me the exact `git add` command (always including
  .claude-logs/, never any env file) and a commit message. I'll commit.
- At the end, push and open a PR with gh. Don't merge: Shakeb reviews it first.

## 1. Audit the codebase

Read the whole repo: the PRD and TRD, the frontend, the FastAPI backend, the
database schema and migrations, the tests, and the existing README, AGENTS.md
and CLAUDE.md.

You may run read-only checks to confirm what actually works (lint, typecheck,
the existing test suites, starting the apps locally). Don't call OpenRouter,
don't write to the shared Supabase project, and never print env values.

Status must come from the code, not the PRD. A feature counts as done only if
it exists and works end to end. Mark everything as one of:
- **Done**: built and working
- **Partial**: started, with what's missing stated
- **Not started**
- **Uses sample data**: UI exists but isn't wired to the API yet

## 2. Write docs/REMAINING_WORK.md

Two sections, Frontend (owner: Nazmul) and Backend (owner: Shakeb), plus a short
"Integration" section for work that needs both of us.

For each item: what it is, its status, the PRD/TRD section it comes from, what
"done" means in one line, priority (must have for the final demo on Thu Nov 5,
or nice to have), and any dependency on the other person.

Order each list by what matters most for the core loop: onboarding → propose an
experiment → daily check-ins → see results and progress. 8x judges "a core loop
that works end to end", so anything blocking that loop goes first.

Also list, separately:
- anything in the PRD/TRD that the code does differently
- bugs or risks you noticed while reading, with file and line
- anything the PRD doesn't cover that the brief's theme needs: a reason to come
  back on day 3 and day 7, and visible progress

Don't invent features. If something isn't in the PRD, TRD or the code, put it
under "Suggestions" and label it as yours.

## 3. Documentation structure, following 8x's templates

Read both templates first:
- https://github.com/8xsocial/template-web
- https://github.com/8xsocial/template-monorepo

Follow their documentation pattern, adapted to our Next.js + FastAPI project.
Don't restructure our code or copy template files we don't use. Create or update:

- **AGENTS.md**: the entry point for every agent. Keep it short: what the
  project is, how to run it, the non-negotiables (session logs committed,
  branch + PR per feature, merge commits only, no secrets in prompts or
  commits), and an index of docs/rules/. Keep everything already in our
  AGENTS.md that's still true.
- **CLAUDE.md**: thin. It includes AGENTS.md and adds only Claude Code
  specifics, including "Always commit `.claude-logs/` with your changes."
- **docs/rules/**: one short file per rule we actually follow (git workflow,
  secrets, API contracts between frontend and backend, testing, UI quality,
  health content: no calorie counting, weight targets or medical claims).
- **docs/ENV_SETUP.md**: every environment variable for frontend and backend,

  include a real value.

  from the code as it is now.

  the PRD and TRD. Don't change or move the PRD or TRD themselves.

Don't change .claude/settings.json, the logging scripts, or .claude-logs/.

## 4. Rewrite the README

Sections:
1. What it is, in two or three sentences, and the "Learning that sticks" angle
2. Team: who owns what (Nazmul: frontend and UI; Shakeb: backend and features)
3. Current status: a short summary, linking to docs/REMAINING_WORK.md

   (Claude Code, Codex)
10. Built during 8x Playmakers C2, by Team F

Keep it short and plain. Every command in it must actually work.

## 5. Hand over

In the PR: what changed, the frontend and backend lists in brief, and anything
you couldn't confirm. Then give me the list of questions for Shakeb: anything in
the backend status you weren't sure about.

Start with step 1.
