2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Next.js 16.4 (App Router)** with `cacheComponents: true` and `partialPrefetching`.
     - Dynamic route params must be read inside `<Suspense>`: `params.then(({id}) => <Comp/>)`, with no `async`/`await params` at page level.
     - `next typegen`; `PageProps<"/route">`, `LayoutProps`.
     - The `next dev` regenerated `AGENTS.md` block lives in `frontend/AGENTS.md`.
     - Partial prerendering shows as `◐` in the build.
   - **Tailwind 4** with `@theme` tokens (oat, paper, ink, muted, line, moss, moss-deep, moss-tint, night, night-tint, sun, clay, clay-tint), and `@tailwindcss/turbopack`.
     - Dev quirk: `globals.css` edits are sometimes missed until a re-save or server restart.
   - **motion 14.7 (`motion/react`):**
     - `MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"`; `useReducedMotion`; `AnimatePresence`; `layoutId`; `useMotionValue`, `animate`, `useTransform`; motion values as children; `useInView`.
     - Pitfalls found:
       - `whileTap` on labels makes them focusable and breaks radios.
       - `pathLength` with `vectorEffect="non-scaling-stroke"` on a stretched SVG breaks dashes.
       - SVG coordinates need rounding to avoid hydration mismatches.
   - **Supabase Auth:** email/password via `lib/auth/supabase.ts` and `SessionProvider` (`useSession()`).
   - **FastAPI backend:**
     - Routes: profile, propose (Jev plus a reasoning call through OpenRouter, about 2 paid calls), save, start, checkins (POST/GET), complete, report.
     - Progress is computed per read. Days are rolling 24h from `started_at`.
     - Completing needs at least min(2, baseline_days) baseline and min(3, intervention_days) test check-ins.
     - Analysis signal classes: likely_signal, possible_signal, unclear, probably_noise. Reason codes as listed in the types.
   - **Error shapes from the backend:**
     - `detail` as a string;
     - FastAPI `[{msg}]`;
     - check-in `[{field, message}]`;
     - an object `{message, baseline_checkins, baseline_required, intervention_checkins, intervention_required}`.
   - **Agent logging:**
     - Hooks write `.claude-logs/<timestamp>_<session>.md`.
     - Codex runs `.codex/hooks.json` → `scripts/extract-log-codex.py`, which reads Codex 0.165 `item_completed` events.
     - Cursor runs `.cursor/hooks.json`.
     - The prompt log lags one turn; the Stop hook catches up.
   - **Testing technique:**
     - Override `window.fetch` in the page to mock writes.
     - Use `window.next.router.push` for soft navigation so mocks persist.
     - Use `sessionStorage` to keep fixtures across a full reload.
     - Generate a realistic report by running the backend's `analyze()` with `uv run python`.

3. Files and Code Sections (latest branch `feat/ui-checkins`; earlier files summarised):

     - `ApiError(status, message, fieldErrors = {}, detail = null)`; status 0 means network failure.
     - `parseDetail` handles the four shapes; check-in field errors give "Some answers need another look." plus `fieldErrors`.

Step 1 is done. I've read the whole repo and run the checks, and the code supports the core loop end to end. The two biggest gaps for the 5 Nov demo are the demo seed and the AI-written parts of the Lab Report. Nothing has been written yet, so there's nothing to commit.

**Branch.** `origin/main` before branching:

```
commit 17a64f4e6e77aa0c035f44cfd9e525cc564fcc72
Merge: 13e93d3 d41b746
Author: Md Nazmul Ahsan
Date:   Thu Oct 29 12:26:55 2026 +0600
    Merge pull request #9 from mnahsanofficial/test/frontend
```

`docs/status-and-readme` is cut from that commit. There are no open PRs, and no remote branch has commits that aren't in main, so I'm not touching anyone's work in progress.

**Checks (read-only).** No OpenRouter calls, no Supabase writes, no env values printed.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Backend `uv run pytest` | 168 passed |
| Frontend `lint`, `typecheck`, `build` | pass |
| Frontend `npm test` | 35 passed (7 files) |
| Frontend `test:e2e` | 2 passed, 2 skipped (marked `test.skip` in the code, with reasons) |

**Status, in core-loop order**

| Area | Frontend | Backend |
|---|---|---|
| Onboarding and profile | Done | Done |
| Sign-in | Email/password done; magic link not started (TRD §6) | JWT check done |
| Propose, prediction, start | Done | Done (Jev, safety, design and prediction) |
| Daily check-in | Done | Done |
| Dashboard and timeline | Done | Done (progress is calculated each time it's read) |
| Finish and Lab Report | Done for the calculated parts; "what we learned" and "next experiment" are placeholders | Analysis done; AI interpretation, learnings and next experiment not started |
| Experiment history | Not started | API exists |
| Seeded demo | Not started | Not started (TRD §28–29) |
| Past experiments feeding predictions | n/a | Not started (PRD §16, TRD §19) |
| Home rhythm ring | Uses sample data, labelled as such on purpose | n/a |

**Where the code differs from the PRD/TRD**
- Experiment design and the prediction come from one AI call. TRD §13 describes two.
- Jev runs through OpenRouter, set by `OPENROUTER_JEV_MODEL`. There are no `JEV_API_KEY` or `JEV_API_URL` variables, and `OPENROUTER_FAST_MODEL` isn't used.
- Days are rolling 24-hour periods from the start time, not calendar days.
- You can finish early once there are 2 baseline and 3 test check-ins. The TRD doesn't cover this.
- The report has no `chart` array (TRD §26); the frontend draws the chart from the check-ins instead.
- Nothing is deployed. The TRD says Vercel and Railway.
- The `draft` status is never used.

**Bugs and risks I found**
1. `components/rhythm-ring.tsx:118`: with reduced motion on, the server and browser render the ring differently (a hydration mismatch). This is why the e2e test at `onboarding-experiment.spec.ts:98` is skipped.
2. `backend/app/services/analysis.py:98` with `components/report/lab-report.tsx:186`: if you finish early, the report lists future days as "Missed".
3. `components/experiments/new-experiment.tsx:~306`: the "you already have an active experiment" check only runs at Start. By then the 2 paid AI calls are already spent.
4. `backend/app/services/progress.py:34`: because days roll over 24 hours from the start time, starting late in the evening shifts every day. Missed days also can't be filled in later.
5. A real experiment can't produce a report before about 3 Nov, so the 5 Nov demo depends on the seed.
6. Frontend tests don't cover check-in, the dashboard or the report. The contract test only checks the profile and experiment schemas.
7. `components/auth-panel.tsx` and `components/backend-status.tsx` aren't used anywhere.
8. Two separate `signedPercent` functions round differently (`new-experiment.tsx:17` and `lib/report/copy.ts`), so the same prediction could show as "+7.5%" on one screen and "+8%" on another.
9. Local to your machine only: lines 1, 3 and 5 of `backend/.env.local` are neither `KEY=value` nor `#` comments, so the env loader warns about them. I checked by key names only, not values.

For the day 3 and day 7 hooks: the dashboard, timeline and "Done for today" give visible progress, and day 4 is when the test phase starts. The PRD has nothing aimed at day 7. I'll put ideas for that under "Suggestions", marked as mine.

Step 3 is written and ready for you to commit. I followed the 8x templates' pattern:
- **One source of truth:** `AGENTS.md` holds the rules, with short summaries that link to one file per rule in `docs/rules/`.
- **A thin `CLAUDE.md`:** it imports `AGENTS.md` and adds only what's specific to Claude Code.
- **Separate docs:** `DESIGN.md`, `PRODUCT.md` and `docs/ENV_SETUP.md`.

I didn't copy any template files we don't use (their CI, Prisma, skills or analytics).

**What's in each file**
- [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md):
  - kept everything from the old one that's still true;
  - added what the project is, how to run it, four non-negotiables and the rules index;
  - fixed one stale line: the backend no longer says "not created yet".
- [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md):
  - imports `AGENTS.md`;
  - adds "Always commit `.claude-logs/` with your changes." and explains why to stage logs last;
  - says to keep the hooks in the project settings, and points at `.claude/launch.json`.
- `docs/rules/`, one short file per rule we already follow:

  - [health-content](docs/rules/health-content.md).
- [docs/ENV_SETUP.md](docs/ENV_SETUP.md): every variable both apps read, taken from the code. It covers names, purpose, defaults and which are secret, with no values. It also notes the two variables the TRD lists that the code doesn't use.
- [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md): the colour, type and shape tokens from `globals.css`, plus the component, layout and motion conventions as built.
- [PRODUCT.md](PRODUCT.md): a one-page summary of the PRD with a "Learning that sticks" section. The PRD and TRD are unchanged.

I've rewritten the [README](README.md) with the 10 sections you asked for and tested every setup and test command in it from a fresh clone of `main`. The old README's setup steps don't work as written: a new clone shows a 500 error on every page.

**What the fresh clone showed**
- **Backend:** `uv sync`, copying the env file and starting uvicorn all work. `/health` returns ok, a protected route correctly returns 401, and `pytest` passes 168 tests. With no env values filled in, `/health/supabase` reports "unavailable".
- **The 500 error:** copying `.env.example` unchanged and running `npm run dev` gives a 500 on every page ("supabaseUrl is required"), and `npm run build` fails the same way.
  - The cause is `frontend/lib/auth/supabase.ts:10`. Its fallback uses `??`, which catches a missing value but not the empty one `.env.example` ships.
  - The app works when both Supabase values are filled in, or when those two lines are deleted.
  - This task is docs only, so I haven't fixed the code. It's now **F0 / R11** in REMAINING_WORK, and changing `??` to `||` fixes it.
  - The README tells people to fill in both values before starting.
  - I also corrected [docs/ENV_SETUP.md](docs/ENV_SETUP.md), which said the app runs fine without them.
- **Install:** `npm install` rewrites `package-lock.json` on this machine's npm version, so the README uses `npm ci`, which doesn't. `npm ci` then `lint`, `typecheck`, `npm test` (35 tests) and `build` all pass.
- **Ports:** your preview servers are using 3000 and 8001, so I ran the test servers on 3200 and 8101. The README gives the normal ports.
- **E2E:** I didn't rerun the Playwright E2E tests in the fresh clone. They passed in the repo earlier today.

I deleted the scratch clone afterwards.

**One thing I couldn't confirm:** the README says the shared Supabase project already has the migrations applied. The app working against it suggests so, but I'll add it to the questions for Shakeb.

```bash
The README now covers what Rhythm is and the "Learning that sticks" angle,

setup, tests, project structure, a documentation index and how we work.

Every command was run from a fresh clone. Copying frontend/.env.example
unchanged makes every page answer 500, because the blank Supabase URL gets
past the `??` fallback in lib/auth/supabase.ts. The README says to fill
those values in first, ENV_SETUP no longer says the app runs without them,
and REMAINING_WORK tracks the bug as F0/R11. The README uses `npm ci` so
setup doesn't rewrite package-lock.json.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
```

Three changes. Frontend only: never edit backend/ or supabase/.

## Workflow

- Check PR #17 (https://github.com/mnahsanofficial/rhythm-health/pull/17).
 It's merged, create a new branch from the latest origin/main: feat/landing-day.
- You write the code. You never commit, push or merge.
- After each chunk, give me the `git add` command (always including
  .claude-logs/, never env files) and a commit message. I'll commit.
- Tests first for anything with behaviour (the password toggle, reduced
  motion), per docs/rules/testing.md.
- In the PR description: what changed and why, how to check it, and
  screenshots of the final result only. **No before/after section.** Remove
  the existing one from PR #17's description if it's still open.

## 1. Landing page: a day in the life, as one long scroll story

The current landing animation doesn't work for me. Replace it with a long
landing page that tells the story of one person's day, driven by scrolling.
The light, the sky and the scene change as you scroll, and the day loops
back to night at the end.

Scenes, in order:
1. **Night:** he sets his alarm, goes to bed, the light switches off. Dark,
   quiet.
2. **Morning:** sunrise, warm light fills everything, he wakes up and starts
   his routine.
3. **Noon:** bright overhead sun, he's at work in the office.
4. **Evening:** low golden light, coffee and a walk.
5. **Night again:** dinner, an evening walk, then he sets his alarm again,
   and the story loops back to scene 1.

Each scene carries one short line about the product, tied to that moment,
so the story explains Docket... I mean Rhythm, as it plays. Examples (use
our real features and the PRD's wording, not these exact lines):
- Night: "Your sleep is the experiment. Test one change at a time."
- Morning: "Check in in 30 seconds: how did you sleep?"
- Evening: "Does a walk after dinner help you sleep? Find out in 7 days."
End with a clear call to action: Start making / Start your first experiment
(match our existing CTA wording).

How to build it:
- Draw everything as original SVG illustration in code: one consistent,
  simple, original character. No stock images, no copied illustrations, no
  known characters.
- Scroll-driven, using Motion's scroll APIs (check current docs). The sky
  colour, light level and sun/moon position change continuously with scroll;
  scene elements move in and out. Use the colour tokens in DESIGN.md: night,
  sun, moss, oat.
- The loop: when the user reaches the end, the scene returns to night, so
  the story reads as a cycle. Don't trap the scroll or hijack it: normal
  scrolling must always work, including on phones.
- Animate transform and opacity only, and keep it smooth on a mid-range
  phone. Keep the page light: no large image files, and lazy-render scenes
  that aren't on screen.
- The text must be real HTML, readable without the animation, so screen
  readers and search engines get the full story.
- With prefers-reduced-motion: show the five scenes as still illustrations,
  one after another, with all the text. No scroll-driven movement.
- Keep the existing sections that explain how the product works below or
  between the scenes, if they still fit. Tell me what you're removing.

Before building, show me in chat: the scene-by-scene plan, the product line
for each scene, and a rough description of the character and style. Wait for
my OK.

## 2. Responsive on every screen size, not just 390px and 1440px

On my Mac the layout leaves empty space on the left and right. Fix this
across every page, not just the landing page:
- Backgrounds, illustrations and full-width sections fill the whole screen
  width at every size.
- Layouts adapt fluidly between breakpoints (fluid sizing, e.g. clamp()),
  not two fixed designs.
- Body text stays at a comfortable reading width (about 65–75 characters
  per line), even on very wide screens. Grids and cards can use the extra
  width; paragraphs shouldn't.
- Check every page at 320, 375, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560px
  wide, and with the browser zoomed to 200%. No horizontal scrolling, no
  overlapping elements, nothing stuck to one side.
- Update DESIGN.md with the layout rules, so every agent follows them.

## 3. Password field on sign-in and sign-up

- The password field must look exactly like the email field: same height,
  border, padding, label and focus style.
- Replace the separate "Show" control with an eye icon inside the field, on
  the right. Clicking it toggles between showing and hiding the password,
  and the icon changes (eye / eye with a line through it).
- It must be a real button: keyboard reachable, aria-label that says
  "Show password" or "Hide password", aria-pressed reflecting the state, a
  44px tap target, and toggling must not move the cursor or lose focus.
- Keep the correct autocomplete attributes (current-password on sign-in,
  new-password on sign-up), so password managers keep working.
- Tests first: the toggle shows and hides the password, the label updates,
  and it works with the keyboard.

Start with the PR #17 check and the scene plan for item 1.

PR #17 merged into main at 11:05 today, so it has nothing left to edit. `feat/landing-day` is cut from `8e87e9b`, the PR #17 merge commit, and carries only the hook's session log change. Here's the plan for item 1.

## The story

He's a 27-year-old software engineer, the persona from the PRD. He's on **day 3 of 10** of his experiment. A small strip of 10 dots in the corner of the scene shows where he is: it starts at day 3 and fills to day 4 when the story loops. That makes the visible-progress and "come back on day 3" ideas concrete.

| # | Scene | What happens in the art | Sky and light | Product line (real HTML) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| — | **Opening**, over the night sky | Nothing yet: the night sky and a lit bedroom window | Night | The `h1` stays: **"Stop guessing what works for you. Test it."** One lead line under it. |
| 1 | **Night** | He sets his phone alarm and lies down. The lamp clicks off, the room goes dark, the moon rises. | `night`, deepest | **Your sleep is the experiment.** Ask one habit question and Rhythm turns it into a small experiment that tests one change at a time. |
| 2 | **Morning** | The sun comes up, warm light fills the room, he sits up and stretches, the kettle goes on. | Night fades to dawn (`sun` light over `oat`) | **Check in in about 30 seconds.** How did you sleep? How's your energy? Did you make the change? |
| 3 | **Noon** | He's at a desk in the office, with the sun high overhead and the window bright. | `oat`, at its brightest; the sun at the top of its arc | **The prediction comes first.** Before day one, Rhythm writes down what it expects, such as "morning energy +8–15%". It's a range, not a promise, and it's locked once you start. |
| 4 | **Evening** | Low golden light. He has a coffee, then walks home past trees. | `sun` low, with long warm light and a `moss` ground | **Late coffee, or none after 2 PM?** Record 3 normal days, then 7 days with one change. |
| 5 | **Night again** | Dinner at the table, a short walk under the first stars, then the alarm again. The lamp goes off and the last frame matches scene 1. | Back to `night` | **Does a walk after dinner help you sleep?** Your Lab Report puts the prediction next to what happened, and says honestly whether it's a likely signal or just noise. |
| — | **Close** | The same night frame, with the dot strip on day 4 | Night | "Tomorrow is day 4." Then **Run your first experiment** (the existing `HomeCta`, wording unchanged) and "About a minute to set up. You sign up at the end." |

The lines are written as questions to test, not health claims. That keeps them within `docs/rules/health-content.md`: no claims that a habit improves sleep, nothing about calories or weight.

## The character and style

- **Him:** an original, flat geometric figure built from rounded shapes, matching `rounded-pill` and `rounded-card`. A round head with two dot eyes and no other face, a `moss` sweater, `ink` trousers. Small and readable, never detailed.
- **Poses:** lying, sitting up, sitting at a desk, walking, holding a cup and setting the alarm. They're all built from the same parts so he always looks like the same person.
- **Scene art:** flat shapes with no outlines, gradients or shadows, in token colours only: `night`, `night-tint`, `sun`, `moss`, `moss-tint`, `oat`, `paper`, `ink` and `line`. It's all inline SVG in code, with no images.

## How it works technically

- **The stage:** a sticky, full-width scene behind the text. On phones it fills the screen with the text cards scrolling over it; from 1024px up, the text runs in a left column at reading width.
- **Scroll:** Motion's `useScroll` and `useTransform`, checked against the 14.7.1 docs and types before I write it.
- **Transform and opacity only:**
  - The sky is four stacked full-width layers (night, dawn, noon, dusk) that crossfade by opacity.
  - Darkness is a `night` overlay whose opacity changes.
  - The sun and moon move along an arc using transforms.
  - Props slide and fade in.
- **Lazy rendering:** only the current scene and its two neighbours are mounted in the stage. The scene index only changes at scene boundaries, not on every scroll frame.
- **Scrolling stays normal:** no snapping, scroll-jacking or locked height. The "loop" is visual: the last frame matches the first, and then the page carries on.
- **The text:** server-rendered HTML in reading order. Each scene is a `section` with an `h2`, and the art is `aria-hidden`.
- **Reduced motion:** five static scene illustrations, one after another, each with its full text. No sticky stage and no scroll hooks.

## What I'd remove

- **`LandingRhythm`**, the "A day, made visible" sample-ring explorer. That's the animation you don't like. I'd delete `components/landing-rhythm.tsx` and rewrite its two tests (`tests/e2e/landing-motion.spec.ts` and the sample assertion in `tests/sample-data/home-sample.test.tsx`) to test the story. `RhythmRing` stays, because onboarding uses it.
- **The "3 + 7" and "30 seconds" stats** below the hero. Their content moves into scenes 2 and 4.

## What I'd keep

**How it works** (the 3 steps) and **A question worth testing** (the 3 examples) stay, placed after the story and before the closing call to action. The signed-in home page doesn't change.

**How it works:**
- The sky, light, sun and moon follow your scroll position, so stopping the scroll stops the scene. Only opacity and transforms change.
- The last frame is the same night as the start (lamp off, asleep), and the "Day 3 of 10" pill turns to day 4.
- Nothing snaps and the scroll is never taken over. A test scrolls to six points in the story and checks the page stays exactly where it was put each time.
- Only the scenes near the current scroll position are rendered.
- All the story text is real HTML, there before any JavaScript runs.
- With reduced motion, the moving scene isn't rendered at all. Each section shows a still illustration above its text.
- The character and every scene are original inline SVG. There are no image files.

That was my mistake. I had already removed the three old files with `git rm`, so they were staged and no longer on disk. The `git add` line then named one of them, failed on that path and staged nothing. So commit `5049998` holds only the three deletions, under a message describing the whole feature.

It hasn't been pushed, so the cleanest fix is to add the rest to that same commit and keep the message:

```bash
git add DESIGN.md frontend/app/globals.css frontend/app/page.tsx frontend/components/landing/ frontend/tests/home/landing-story.test.tsx frontend/tests/e2e/landing-story.spec.ts .claude-logs/
```

```bash
git commit --amend --no-edit
```

`--amend` only rewrites your local, unpushed commit, so nothing on GitHub changes. If you'd rather not amend, run the same `git add` and then a second commit instead:

```bash
git commit -m "feat(frontend): add the landing day story scenes and tests" -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

From now on I'll only list paths that still exist on disk in the `git add` commands.

Chunk 2 is ready: every page now uses the full screen width at any size, and paragraphs stop at a readable line length. All checks pass:

- **Browser tests:** 49 pass. The 1 skip was already in the suite before this change.
- **Unit tests:** all 69 pass.
- **Lint and typecheck:** clean.

**Why your Mac showed empty sides:** each page centred its own fixed container, from `max-w-6xl` (1152px) down to `max-w-3xl`. On a 1440–1512px screen that left 150–250px blank on each side.

**What changed:**
- **One shared page frame.** Every page, and the header, now uses a single `page-frame` class. It fills the width up to 90rem of content and is centred. Side spacing grows with the screen, from 16px on phones to 64px.
  - On a 1440px screen, content now spans about 1310px. On 2560px it stays centred, so nothing hugs one side.
  - Focused screens stay narrower on purpose: check-in, sign-in and the demo page.
- **Readable paragraphs.** A global rule stops every paragraph at about 65–75 characters a line. I removed the `max-w-2xl` classes that were overriding it, mostly in the Lab Report. Cards, timelines and grids still use the full width.
- **Fluid headings.** Heading sizes change smoothly with the screen width instead of jumping at one breakpoint, and they still grow with browser zoom.
- **Landing story.** The text cards now line up with the header logo at every width. On a very wide screen they were hugging the left edge.
- **Phone action bars.** The sticky Save and Continue bars stretch to the screen edge at any side spacing, instead of assuming 16px.

**The new layout test** (`layout-widths.spec.ts`) visits every page at:
- 320, 375, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560px;
- 1280px and 1440px windows zoomed to 200%.

It fails on horizontal scrolling, content off centre, a frame not using the available width, a paragraph over 76 characters a line, or text and controls overlapping. I ran it before changing any layout, and it failed for the expected reasons. It skips content scrolling under the phone's sticky action bars, which is by design.

**DESIGN.md** has a new **Layout** section with these rules and how to run the test. `docs/rules/ui-quality.md` now points to it.

**Chunk 2 commit:**

```bash
git add DESIGN.md docs/rules/ui-quality.md frontend/app/demo/page.tsx frontend/app/experiments/new/page.tsx frontend/app/globals.css frontend/app/sign-in/page.tsx frontend/components/checkins/checkin-form.tsx frontend/components/experiments/experiment-detail.tsx frontend/components/experiments/experiment-history.tsx frontend/components/experiments/new-experiment.tsx frontend/components/home-experience.tsx frontend/components/landing/day-story.tsx frontend/components/onboarding/onboarding-flow.tsx frontend/components/report/lab-report.tsx frontend/components/site-header.tsx frontend/tests/e2e/layout-widths.spec.ts .claude-logs/
```

```
fluid clamp() sizes. Add a layout test that checks every page at nine
widths and at 200% zoom for sideways scroll, off-centre content,
```

The password field on sign-in and sign-up now looks like the email field, with an eye icon inside it on the right that shows or hides the password.

**The toggle:**
- **Matching style:** both fields share one style. The only difference is extra right padding on the password field, so typed text doesn't run under the icon.
- **Eye icon:** it's an open eye while the password is hidden and a crossed-out eye while it's shown.
- **A real button:**
  - Tab reaches it, and Enter or Space toggles it.
  - Its name switches between "Show password" and "Hide password", `aria-pressed` follows the state, and it points at the field with `aria-controls`.
  - The tap target is 44×44px.
- **Focus and cursor:** clicking the eye while typing keeps focus and the cursor in the field, at the same position. Using the keyboard, focus stays on the eye.
- **Password managers:** sign-in still uses `current-password` and sign-up `new-password`.

**Tests:** I wrote these before changing the form, and each failed for the expected reason first.
- `password-toggle.test.tsx` (unit): show and hide, the label and pressed state, the keyboard, the cursor position, icon only, matching styles, and autocomplete.
- `password-field.spec.ts` (browser, at 320, 390 and 1440px):
  - same height, border, padding, label and focus ring as email;
  - the eye is at least 44px and sits inside the field;
  - the cursor survives a click;
  - Tab and Enter work.

**Results:** lint and typecheck are clean and all 76 unit tests pass. In the browser suite, everything passes apart from the 1 skip that was already there. One test needed a fix after the full run: the layout test flagged the eye as overlapping the password field. I taught it that a control drawn inside the field it controls is meant to overlap, and reran it.

**Also changed:** one line in the shared test helper. It now finds the password field with `getByLabel("Password", { exact: true })`, because "Show password" would otherwise also match. In the browser pane I only clicked the eye on the empty field; I never typed into the real sign-in form.

The illustration is back, and the header now stays at the top of every page. Signed-in people get a proper nav and an account menu.

**Why you saw no illustration.** The dev server on port 3000 was serving an old copy of `globals.css`. It had picked up the new layout classes but not the `--header-h` variable I added with the header. Without that variable the scene's height worked out to 0px, so only the text cards showed. The test browser builds fresh on port 3100, so every test passed and missed it. I restarted the server, and the scene now sits below the header and plays as you scroll. Reload your tab to get the fresh CSS.

If you still see no scene after reloading, check that you're signed out. When signed in, `/` shows "Your lab" instead of the story, by design.

**The console error isn't from our code.** The only mismatch is `cz-shortcut-listen="true"` on `<body>`, which the ColorZilla browser extension adds before React loads. It disappears in a window without extensions. I've left it alone rather than add `suppressHydrationWarning`, which would also hide real mismatches on that element.

**The header:**
- **Fixed:** it stays at the top on every page, with a solid background and a thin border. The landing scene, the onboarding summary card and focused headings all start below it rather than underneath.
- **Visitors** still see the logo and Sign in.
- **Signed in, desktop:** the logo, then Your lab · History · Demo. The current page has a green underline and is announced as the current page. Experiment pages count as Your lab.
- **Account button:** shows your initial. It opens a panel with your email, Update your rhythm and Sign out. Escape or a click outside closes it and puts focus back on the button.
- **Phones:** the bar holds just the logo and one 44px button, and the panel also lists the three sections.

**Tests.** I wrote the new ones first and they failed before I built anything:
- unit: 7 header tests;
- browser: one at 320, 390 and 1440px that checks the header stays at the top while scrolling, plus the phone and desktop menus and keyboard use.

I also updated two existing tests: Sign out now sits behind the account button, and the demo test now finds the Demo link inside the page's own nav. Lint and typecheck are clean, all 83 unit tests pass, and 55 browser tests pass with 1 skip that was already there. DESIGN.md now has the fixed-header rules.

```bash
The header now stays at the top of every page, with a solid
background and a bottom border. Its height lives in --header-h,
so the landing scene, the onboarding summary and focused headings
sit below it. Signed-in people get the main sections (Your lab,
```

## Error Type
Console Error

## Error Message
A tree hydrated but some attributes of the server rendered HTML didn't match the client properties. This won't be patched up. This can happen if a SSR-ed Client Component used:

- A server/client branch `if (typeof window !== 'undefined')`.
- Variable input such as `Date.now()` or `Math.random()` which changes each time it's called.
- Date formatting in a user's locale which doesn't match the server.
- External changing data without sending a snapshot of it along with the HTML.
- Invalid HTML tag nesting.

It can also happen if the client has a browser extension installed which messes with the HTML before React loaded.

https://react.dev/link/hydration-mismatch

I've fixed that warning. The ColorZilla extension adds `cz-shortcut-listen="true"` to `<body>` before React loads, and React reported that as a mismatch. Adding `suppressHydrationWarning` to `<body>` in `frontend/app/layout.tsx` stops it. That only ignores attribute differences on `<body>` itself; mismatches anywhere inside the page are still reported.

**Tests:** a new browser test, `extension-attributes.spec.ts`, adds the attribute the moment `<body>` exists, the same way the extension does. My first version passed even without the fix, so it wasn't really testing anything. I rewrote it until it failed with your exact warning, then applied the fix and it passed. Lint, typecheck and all 83 unit tests pass, and the landing and onboarding browser tests still catch real mismatches.

**New branch:** PR #18 had already been merged into main with a merge commit, so I moved the change to `fix/body-extension-attributes`, cut from the latest main (`aa0570e`). Nothing is committed or pushed.

**1. Commit:**

```bash
git add frontend/app/layout.tsx frontend/tests/e2e/extension-attributes.spec.ts .claude-logs/
```

```bash
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
fix(frontend): ignore extension attributes on <body> during hydration

Browser extensions such as ColorZilla add attributes like
cz-shortcut-listen to <body> before React loads, which showed as a
hydration mismatch in the dev console. suppressHydrationWarning on
<body> ignores attribute differences on that element only;
mismatches inside the page are still reported. A new e2e test
mimics the extension and fails on any hydration warning.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
```

**2. Push:**

```bash
git push -u origin fix/body-extension-attributes
```

**3. Open the PR:**

```bash
gh pr create -R mnahsanofficial/rhythm-health --base main --head fix/body-extension-attributes --title "fix(frontend): ignore extension attributes on <body> during hydration" --body-file /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-nazmulahsan-Health/94c5e335-69b1-4428-8f4e-8c2f5a5aa074/scratchpad/pr-body-extension.md
```

Route "/experiments/[id]/check-in": Next.js encountered URL data `usePathname()` in a Client Component outside of `<Suspense>`.

Ways to fix this:

  - [block] Set `export const instant = false` to allow a blocking route

I've fixed the "Blocking Route" error on the check-in page. It came from my header change in #18, and it also made `npm run build` fail. I didn't run the build before that PR; it would have caught this.

**Cause:** the header's nav calls `usePathname()` to mark the current page, and the header renders on every page. With Cache Components on, `usePathname()` waits on pages whose `[id]` isn't known at build time. Without a `<Suspense>` boundary, Next.js can't prerender `/experiments/[id]`, `/experiments/[id]/check-in` or `/experiments/[id]/report`.

**Fix:** `SiteHeader` now wraps the nav in `<Suspense>`, as the Next.js docs recommend for this case. The rest of the header still prerenders and the nav streams in. While it loads, it shows the same placeholder as before, so nothing shifts.

**Checked:**
- The build failed before the change (`Export encountered an error on /experiments/[id]/page`) and passes now. The three experiment routes build as partially prerendered.
- Lint and typecheck are clean, all 83 unit tests pass, and 56 browser tests pass, plus the 1 skip that was already there.
- On `/experiments/8a89e4fb-…/check-in` in the dev server the error is gone. The pane isn't signed in, so I couldn't see the signed-in page itself; it redirected to sign-in as expected.

The fix is on `fix/header-pathname-suspense`, cut from the latest main (`8769ecc`, after #19). Nothing is committed or pushed.

**1. Commit:**

```bash
git add frontend/components/site-header.tsx .claude-logs/
```

```bash
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
fix(frontend): stream the header nav under Suspense

The header nav reads usePathname() to mark the current page. With
Cache Components, that suspends on /experiments/[id] routes, whose id
isn't known at build time. Without a boundary those pages couldn't
prerender: a Blocking Route error in dev and a failed build. Wrap
AuthNav in Suspense, with the nav's loading placeholder as the
fallback, so the rest of the header prerenders.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
```

**2. Push:**

```bash
git push -u origin fix/header-pathname-suspense
```

**3. Open the PR:**

```bash
gh pr create -R mnahsanofficial/rhythm-health --base main --head fix/header-pathname-suspense --title "fix(frontend): stream the header nav under Suspense" --body-file /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-nazmulahsan-Health/94c5e335-69b1-4428-8f4e-8c2f5a5aa074/scratchpad/pr-body-header-suspense.md
```

Shakeb's PR #21 is merged into main:
https://github.com/mnahsanofficial/rhythm-health/pull/21
It changes the Lab Report API, and the frontend still reads the old format.
Update the frontend to match. Frontend only: never edit backend/ or supabase/.

## Workflow

- git fetch, then a new branch from the latest origin/main: feat/lab-report-v2.
  If my landing-page branch isn't merged yet, keep this work separate from it.
- You write the code. You never commit, push or merge.
- Tests first (red, then green), per docs/rules/testing.md.
- After each chunk, give me the `git add` command (always including
  .claude-logs/, never env files) and a commit message. I'll commit.
- At the end, open a PR with gh. No before/after section. Don't merge.

## 1. Read before changing anything

- Read PR #21, docs/REMAINING_WORK.md (F2, F3, F4, I2, I6, R12) and
  docs/DECISIONS.md, then the actual backend code for the report endpoints.
  The code is the truth.
- Confirm the new shape. What I understand from the PR:
  - /complete, GET /report and POST /report all return a LabReport:
    {experiment, analysis, narrative | null, chart, created_at}
  - narrative has: summary, interpretation, personal learnings,
    confounders, next experiment
  - chart is chart-ready data
  - POST /report generates the narrative with one paid model call; it's
    idempotent unless ?regenerate=true
  - /complete is deterministic and makes no model call
- Check whether these are implemented in the backend now, or only decided:
  the B1 demo reset (returns a bare LabReport) and the B4 missing-days rule.
- Show me in chat: the exact types, every frontend place that reads the old
  shape, and your plan. Wait for my OK.

## 2. Refresh the API contract (I6)

frontend/tests/contracts/openapi.json is stale. Regenerate it from the
running backend's OpenAPI schema, then update the frontend types and MSW
mocks from it. Existing tests that break because the contract changed are
the to-do list: fix the code, not the tests, unless a test asserts the old
shape.

## 3. Wire the Lab Report (F3, I2)

- Every report read uses the new LabReport shape, including the response
  from /complete.
- Show the narrative sections: summary, interpretation, personal learnings,
  confounders, next experiment.
- **Narrative is null until generated.** After completion, show the
  analysis straight away, then a clear action to generate the written
  report, calling POST /report.
  - It's a paid call: disable the button while it runs, show what's
    happening, and never fire it twice on double-click or re-render.
  - Errors say what went wrong and offer a retry.
  - Don't expose ?regenerate=true in the UI. Tell me if you think we need it.
- **Numbers come from `analysis`, never from the narrative text.** PR #21
  says narrative numbers aren't checked against the analysis (R12). The
  prediction vs reality reveal and every figure on the page must come from
  `analysis`.
- Build the day chart from the new `chart` data, replacing any chart maths
  the frontend does itself.
- **Next experiment → "Run this experiment":** prefill the propose flow with
  it. Check for an active experiment first (F1) and show the message
  instead if one is running.
- Keep our design system and motion rules: the prediction vs reality reveal
  stays the signature moment, and reduced motion still works.

## 4. Other items, if the backend supports them

- **F4 missing days:** if B4 is implemented, the report shows exactly what
  the backend returns. Add tests that a future day is never shown as missed
  after an early finish.
- **F2 demo:** if the B1 demo reset endpoint exists, add the entry point to
  open the seeded experiment and its report. If it doesn't exist yet, skip it
  and tell me.

## 5. Check and hand over

- Run the full frontend test suite three times, plus lint and typecheck.
- Mock every paid call in tests. To prove the real flow works, make at most
  one real POST /report call by hand, with my test account, and tell me you
  did.
- Walk the flow in a browser at mobile, tablet and desktop widths: complete
  an experiment → see the analysis → generate the report → read the
  narrative → "Run this experiment".
- Update docs/REMAINING_WORK.md (F3, I2, I6, and F2/F4 if done).
- In the PR: what's wired, what's still waiting on the backend, and any

Step 1 is done and nothing has changed in the code yet. The branch `feat/lab-report-v2` is cut from main at `4db762d`, the PR #21 merge commit. All my earlier PRs, including #20, are merged, so there was nothing to keep separate.

## The shape, confirmed from the backend code

Your reading of PR #21 is correct. I checked it against `backend/app/schemas/lab_report.py`, `api/reports.py` and `services/lab_report.py`.

| Call | Returns | Model call |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /complete` | `LabReport` with `narrative: null` | None, deterministic |
| `GET /report` | `LabReport`; `narrative` is null until written | None |
| `POST /report` | `LabReport` with the narrative filled | One paid call (plus at most one retry). Returns the stored narrative without calling the model, unless `?regenerate=true` |

`POST /report` errors: 409 (not completed), 404 (no report), 503 (AI unavailable), 502 (output still invalid after the retry).

These are the frontend types I'll write. They match the backend models, and nullable fields come back as `null`.

```ts
type LabReport = {
  experiment: Experiment;              // embedded, so no separate experiment fetch
  analysis: Analysis;                  // unchanged, and the only source of numbers
  narrative: ReportNarrative | null;
  chart: ChartData;
  created_at: string;
};
type ReportNarrative = {
  summary: string; interpretation: string;
  personal_learnings: string[]; confounders: string[];
  next_experiment: { question: string; reason: string };
};
type ChartMetric = { key: string; label: string; type: string; min: number | null; max: number | null };
type ChartPoint = {
  day: number; phase: string; logged: boolean;
  intervention_completed: boolean | null;
  value: number | null;                // primary metric
  values: Record<string, MetricValue> | null;
  note: string | null;
};
type PredictedBand = {
  min_percent: number; max_percent: number; midpoint_percent: number;
  expected_value_min: number; expected_value_max: number;
};
type ChartData = {
  primary_metric: ChartMetric; metrics: ChartMetric[];
  baseline_days: number; intervention_days: number;
  points: ChartPoint[];                // one per planned day, logged or not
  baseline_mean: number | null; intervention_mean: number | null;
  predicted_band: PredictedBand | null;
};
```

## B1 and B4 are decided but not built

- **B1, demo reset:** there's no `/api/demo/*` route in `backend/app/api/`. The contract is decided: it will return the bare `LabReport`.
- **B4, missing days:** `analysis.py:98` still lists every unlogged day up to the planned total. That includes future days after an early finish, and `coverage_rate` still divides by every planned day.

## Every place that reads the old shape

| File | What it reads |
|---|---|
| `lib/types/api.ts` | `Report` (flat `interpretation`, `learnings`, `next_experiment`, `experiment_id`) and `CompleteResponse` (`{experiment, report}`) |
| `lib/data/reports.ts` | `getReport` and `completeExperiment` are typed to the old shapes. There's no `POST /report` call at all |
| `lib/data/demo.ts`, `components/demo-entry.tsx` | Expect the `{experiment, report}` wrapper |
| `components/report/lab-report.tsx` | `reportNarrative()` looks for the narrative at the top level of the report, so it never finds it. The page also fetches the experiment and check-ins separately |
| `components/report/day-chart.tsx` | Computes its own predicted band from the baseline mean and builds its points from raw check-ins |
| `components/experiments/experiment-history.tsx` | `Report` type only; it reads `analysis`, which keeps the same path |
| `components/experiments/experiment-detail.tsx` | Calls `/complete` and ignores the body, then opens the report. Only its type changes |
| Tests and mocks | `tests/report/lab-report-learning.test.tsx`, `prediction-reveal.test.tsx`, `tests/data/demo.test.ts`, and the e2e specs `lab-report-learning`, `experiment-history`, `seeded-demo`, `layout-widths`. There's no shared report fixture, and `openapi.json` still has `ReportOut` |

## Plan, in chunks, tests first

**1. Contract (I6).**
- Regenerate `openapi.json` from the running backend's `/openapi.json`. That's a schema read only: no OpenRouter call and no Supabase write.
- Add one shared `labReport` fixture (narrative `null`) and one `writtenLabReport` (narrative filled). The contract test checks both against `LabReport`; it should fail first.
- Then replace the old types, update the data layer and MSW mocks, and fix whatever breaks.

**2. Lab Report (F3, I2).**
- **One call:** the page makes a single `GET /report`. It fetches the experiment only on a 404, to show the "not finished yet" state.
- **Analysis first:** the comparison, verdict, adherence, chart and other metrics all render straight away from `analysis` and `chart`.
- **`narrative: null`:** a "Write my report" button calls `POST /report`. While it runs, the button is disabled with a busy label and a polite status explaining what's happening. A ref guard stops a double-click or re-render from firing it twice.
- **Errors:** 503, 502, 409 and 404 each get plain wording and Try again. Any other failure gets a generic message, also with Try again.
- **Narrative:** summary, interpretation, personal learnings, confounders and next experiment. Every figure on the page comes from `analysis`, never parsed from the narrative text (R12).
- **Day chart:** points and the predicted band come from `chart`. I'll delete the frontend's own band maths.
- **Run this experiment:** links to `/experiments/new?question=…`. The report checks for an active experiment first and shows "finish your current experiment" with a link instead of the button. The proposal page keeps its own check before the paid call.
- **Kept:** the prediction-vs-reality reveal stays the signature moment, and reduced motion still works.

**3. Missing days (F4) and demo (F2).**
- **F4:** "Missed" and the chart both come from `analysis.adherence.missing_days`, with no rule of our own. An unlogged day that isn't in that list shows as "not reached", never "missed".
  - The tests use an early-finish fixture with `missing_days: [4]` and days 8–10 unlogged, and assert days 8–10 never say "missed". Today's chart calls every unlogged day missed, so that test will fail first.
  - Until B4 lands, the live backend still lists future days as missing. The UI will show them as returned.
- **F2:** I'll skip the entry point, as you asked. The entry was already built in #16 and calls the not-yet-built endpoint, so clicking it today shows an error. I'll switch its type to the bare `LabReport` so it's ready for B1.

**4. Check and hand over.** The full suite three times, plus lint, typecheck and the build. A browser walk at 390, 768 and 1440px. At most one real `POST /report` call. Then `REMAINING_WORK.md` and the PR.

## `?regenerate=true`

I'd leave it out of the UI. Each use is another paid call and replaces the person's written report. The narrative is already checked for over-claiming and signal agreement, and nothing in the PRD asks for a rewrite. If a narrative ever comes out wrong during the demo, Shakeb can regenerate it from the API.

## Three decisions I need from you

1. **Demo entry:** hide it until B1 exists, or leave it as is? Leaving it means clicking it shows an error until B1 is built. I'd hide it.
2. **The one real `POST /report` call:** you'll sign in to your test account in the pane; I never type passwords. Which experiment should it use? It needs one that's already completed, or one with enough check-ins to finish. I won't add made-up check-ins to a real account.
3. **The chart:** `chart.points` has no "elapsed" flag. I'll use `missing_days` to tell missed days from days not reached; that's the same field, so there's still no second rule. Questions for Shakeb in the PR:
   - Could a point carry `missed: boolean`, or could `chart` carry an `elapsed_days` count?
   - When are B1 and B4 expected?

Chunk 2 is ready to commit. The Lab Report now uses the new data from start to finish. The production build and all 101 unit tests pass, and in the full browser suite 59 pass with 1 skip that was already there.

**What changed:**
- **One call:** the page makes a single `GET /report` and shows the comparison, verdict, adherence, chart and other metrics straight away. It fetches the experiment only when there's no report yet, to show "No results yet."
- **Write my report:**
  - Clicking it sends `POST /report` once. A ref guard stops a double-click or a re-render from sending it twice.
  - While it runs, the button reads "Writing your report…", disabled, and a polite status explains what's happening.
  - Once written, focus moves to "What you learned about you". A reload reads the stored narrative and doesn't write it again.
- **Errors:**
  - 503 says the writing service isn't available right now.
  - 502 says we couldn't write a report that matched your results.
  - A lost connection says so, and anything else shows the server's message.
  - Each of these also says the results above are saved and offers Try again.
  - 409 and 404 explain the problem without a retry.
- **Narrative:** summary, interpretation, personal learnings, confounders and next experiment, with a note that the figures come from the check-ins.
- **Figures:** they all come from `analysis`. A test gives the narrative a wrong "50%" and checks it appears nowhere outside the narrative (R12).
- **Day chart:** drawn from `chart.points` and `chart.predicted_band`; the frontend's own band maths is deleted. The predicted range is now shown in numbers, for example "5.8–6.1".
- **Missed versus not reached (F4):** a day counts as missed only if it's in `analysis.adherence.missing_days`. Any other unlogged day is "Not reached before you finished". Until B4 lands, the live backend still lists future days in `missing_days`, so they'll show as missed, exactly as returned.
- **Run this experiment:**
  - The report checks for an active experiment first.
  - If one is running, it shows "You're already running an experiment" and a link to it instead of the button.
  - If the check fails, the button still shows, because the proposal page checks again before its paid call.
  - Before the narrative exists, it offers "Ask your own question".
- **Design rules kept:** the prediction-vs-reality reveal is unchanged and still the signature moment. No motion was added to the waiting state.

**Tests, written first:**
- `tests/report/lab-report-flow.test.tsx`, 10 tests, all failing before the change: one call only, the double-click guard and busy state, 503 and 502 errors with a working retry, figures from the analysis, both "Run this experiment" paths, no report yet, and the chart from `chart` with missed versus not reached.
- `tests/e2e/report-writing.spec.ts` at 390, 768 and 1440px: see the analysis, double-click Write, check the busy state, read the narrative, reload with no second call, then Run this experiment opens a prefilled proposal with no proposal call made.
- I updated two existing tests:
  - the older learning unit test needed a mock for the new active-experiment check, and now waits for the link;
  - one reveal spec line expected the old "Waiting for your written results" text, and now expects the Write button.

```bash
git add frontend/components/report/day-chart.tsx frontend/components/report/lab-report.tsx frontend/tests/e2e/lab-report-learning.spec.ts frontend/tests/e2e/report-writing.spec.ts frontend/tests/report/lab-report-learning.test.tsx frontend/tests/report/lab-report-flow.test.tsx .claude-logs/
```

```bash
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
feat(frontend): wire the Lab Report to analysis, chart and narrative

The report loads with one GET /report and shows the analysis straight
away. While the narrative is null, Write my report calls POST /report
once: a ref guard blocks double-clicks, the busy state is announced,
and 503/502 errors explain what failed and offer a retry. The
narrative sections render from report.narrative; every figure comes
from analysis. The day chart uses chart.points and predicted_band
instead of its own maths, and only calls a day missed when
missing_days says so. Run this experiment prefills the proposal, or
points to the running experiment if one is active.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
```

# Rhythm: 10-minute demo script

This is based on what's merged on main today, up to PR #26. PR #27 (architecture docs) is still open, so I haven't counted it.

## Before you start (5 minutes beforehand)

- **Servers:**
  - frontend: `cd frontend && npm run dev`, on port 3000;
  - backend: `cd backend && uv run uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8001`;
  - check `http://localhost:8001/health/supabase` returns `{"status":"ok"}`.
- **Tabs:**
  - a signed-out tab on `/`, scrolled to the top, for the landing story;
  - a tab signed in to your account. Its active experiment is on day 1 with one check-in;
  - in that account, open **Demo** and click **Explore a completed example** once, so the seeded report is ready.
- **Phone view:** the daily loop is mobile-first, so use DevTools' 390px view for the check-in part.
- **Backup:** if the network fails, use the screenshots in `docs/screenshots/landing-day/` and `docs/screenshots/lab-report-v2/`.
- **Paid calls:** none in this script. Your account already has an active experiment, so the product itself blocks a new paid proposal; that's a feature to show (step 3). The seeded report already has its narrative written.

## The script

### 0:00–1:00 · The problem and the product (signed-out landing)

> "Most health advice is about people in general. Rhythm answers one question about **you**: does this habit actually change anything for me?
>
> You ask a question like *'Does walking after dinner help me sleep?'* Rhythm turns it into a short experiment and **predicts the result before you start**. You log a 30-second check-in each day: 3 normal days, then 7 days with one change. At the end, a Lab Report puts the prediction next to what actually happened, and says honestly whether it's a signal or just noise."

Scroll the landing story slowly. The sky moves from night to morning, noon and evening, and back to night.

> "The landing page tells that story as one day. Each moment explains one part of the product."

### 1:00–2:00 · Learning that sticks

> "Our theme is *Learning that sticks*. Three things make that happen:
> 1. **The prediction comes first.** The result is then a surprise or a confirmation, which you remember.
> 2. **You do something every day.** A 30-second check-in, not something to read.
> 3. **The evidence is about you.** It's built from your own days, so the lesson is personal."

### 2:00–3:30 · Your lab and the daily check-in (signed in, 390px)

Open **Your lab**.

> "This is the daily home: my question, the one small change, what day it is, and today's check-in."

Point at:
- "Day 1 of 10";
- **Done for today**, and when day 2 opens;
- the 10-day timeline.

> "Days are rolling 24 hours from when you started. The timeline fills in as evidence builds: that's visible progress, and the reason to come back on day 3 and day 7."

Open the experiment page and point at **the original forecast card**:

> "The prediction was made before day one and is now locked. It's a range, not a promise."

### 3:30–4:15 · Guard rails (still signed in)

Back on Your lab, click **Test another habit**.

> "You can line up your next question from here, but you can only run one experiment at a time, so each result stays clear. Rhythm checks before it makes any paid AI call."

If someone asks about safety:

> "Questions are checked for risk first, and it never does calorie counting, weight targets or medical claims. Risky ideas get a safer alternative instead."

### 4:15–7:15 · The Lab Report (Demo → seeded example)

Open **Demo**, then **Explore a completed example**.

> "Since a real experiment takes 10 days, here's a seeded one that's already finished. It's labelled as a sample, and it runs through exactly the same screens and tables."

1. **Prediction vs reality** (the signature moment). Click **Reveal my result**.
   > "We predicted this range. Here's what you actually saw, and how close the forecast came."
2. **Signal or noise.**
   > "It's a likely signal, a possible signal, unclear, or probably noise, with the reasons in plain words. A short personal experiment shows a pattern, not proof."
3. **How the days went:** adherence, missed days and coverage.
   > "If you finish early, only days that had already passed count as missed. Future days don't."
4. **Day by day.** Tap a day.
   > "Every check-in, the baseline average and the predicted range. Tap a day to see your answers and your note."
5. **What you learned about you.**
   > "The AI writes the explanation: a summary, personal learnings, and what may have shaped the result. But **every number on this page comes from the deterministic analysis, never from the AI's text.** The rule is: AI proposes, Python measures."
6. **What to test next**, then **Run this experiment**.
   > "Each experiment suggests the next one, and it's prefilled. The new prediction also uses your past completed experiments as context, so the predictions get more personal over time."

### 7:15–7:45 · History

Open **History**.

> "Every completed experiment, with its prediction, what was observed and the signal. Over time this becomes your personal evidence base."

### 7:45–8:30 · How it's built (one slide or just say it)

> "A Next.js frontend and a FastAPI backend on Supabase, with OpenRouter for the AI.
> - The AI designs the experiment and writes the explanation.
> - Python does all the analysis: means, effect size, consistency and the verdict.
> - Writing the report is one paid call, about 10 seconds. It's only made when you ask, and it's never sent twice.
> - Tested: 218 backend tests, 121 frontend tests and 70 browser tests, run at phone, tablet and desktop widths."

### 8:30–9:30 · What's done and what's left

| Done and working | Still to do |
|---|---|
| Onboarding, then your rhythm | **Primary-metric trend on the dashboard** (partly done; today the trend is only in the report) |
| Ask a question, then a safety check, then a prediction, then start | **Edit the AI's proposal before starting** (not started) |
| Daily 30-second check-in | **Share or export the Lab Report** (not started) |
| Dashboard and timeline with phases, missed and upcoming days | **A "missed" flag on each chart day** from the backend (agreed, not built; the result is already correct) |
| Finishing early, with correct missed days | **Regenerate the prediction before start** (not started) |
| Lab Report: prediction vs reality, signal, chart, AI narrative, next experiment | **One full-flow integration test** (planned as the last step) |
| Past experiments as context for new predictions | Logging and an optional extra AI safety check (deferred, if time permits) |
| Seeded demo and experiment history | **Deployment**: everything runs on localhost for C2, by decision |
| Fixed header, fluid layout from 320px to 2560px, accessible password field | A live early-finish check on a real account (verified with isolated data) |

> "The core loop works end to end. What's left is polish and nice-to-haves before the final demo on 5 November."

### 9:30–10:00 · Close

> "Rhythm doesn't tell you what works for people in general. It helps you find out what works for **you**, one small, honest experiment at a time. Thank you. Happy to take questions."

## Likely questions, with short answers

- **"Is this medical advice?"** No. There's no calorie counting, weight targets or medical claims, and the report says a short experiment shows a pattern, not proof.
- **"What if the AI makes up a number?"** It can't change the figures on the page. Every number comes from the analysis, and the AI's text is checked so it can't claim proof or contradict the verdict.
- **"Why not just show the data?"** Making the prediction first is the learning part. Comparing your expectation with what really happened is what makes it stick.
- **"Who built what?"** Nazmul built the frontend and UI. Shakeb built the backend, the AI and the analysis.

If you'd like this as a page you can share with Shakeb or keep open while you present, I can publish it as one.

## 1. Feedback we've received so far

FILL IN BEFORE SENDING: paste the reviewers' feedback from the session, word
for word. If there isn't any yet, delete this section.

- what they most likely mean, with evidence from our code or UI
- two or three concrete changes that would address it, smallest first
- which you'd do first and why

## 2. Make the UI unmistakably ours, not AI-default

My takeaways from 8x's design workshop:
FILL IN BEFORE SENDING: your three takeaways, in your own words.

Audit every screen against the patterns that make a UI look AI-generated:
- default fonts and type scales with no real hierarchy
- gradients, glows or glass used as decoration
- the same rounded card with the same shadow everywhere
- emoji or generic icon sets standing in for real visuals
- generic copy ("Unlock your potential", "Transform your health") instead of
  plain, specific words about sleep, food and movement
- everything centred, everything the same size, nothing leading the eye
- motion on everything, instead of motion that answers an action

For each one you find: the screen, file and line, why it reads as generic,
and the fix, consistent with DESIGN.md. Compare with
https://www.karma.business/ for the quality bar, not the look. Show me the
list with screenshots and wait for my OK, then fix them screen by screen,
starting with the screens the demo shows first.

## 3. Architecture and code quality (scored automatically)

Review the frontend code as a senior reviewer would:
- structure: components, data layer, types, where logic lives
- duplication, dead code, leftover sample data or mocks in the real flow
- type safety: any `any`, unchecked API responses, missing error handling
- consistency with docs/ARCHITECTURE.md and docs/rules/

Give me a short ranked list: the problem, the file, the risk, and the fix.
Only small, safe refactors, each covered by tests. No big rewrites this
close to the review. Update docs/ARCHITECTURE.md if anything changes.

## 4. After each finished feature

End with a short handover I'll turn into my own update and demo:
- what changed and why, in three bullets
- any issue you hit and how it was solved
- a demo script under 2 minutes: what to click, in what order, and the one
  sentence that explains why the feature matters to the user

Start with section 1.

I haven't removed anything, and I'd advise against it, for two reasons:

1. **It breaks a repo rule.** The first non-negotiable in `AGENTS.md` says never to "gitignore it, edit it or delete files from it" for `.claude-logs/`. The logs are the evidence 8x uses to rate each of you individually. A log with turns cut out could read as tampering, which is worse than having the turns there.
2. **It wouldn't last.** The hook rebuilds this session's log file from the whole conversation at the end of every turn (`docs/logging.md`). Anything I deleted would come back as soon as this turn ends, this turn included.

Those turns are also harmless:
- **The demo script:** a summary of what's built and what's left, already public in `REMAINING_WORK.md`.
- **The review brief:** the instructions, with its "fill in" placeholders.
- **Secrets:** neither has a secret value, env value or password.

If something specific in them worries you, tell me which part and I'll say plainly whether it's actually a problem. If the worry is how the unfilled brief looks, the next turn will show it being completed, which reads fine. Changing the logging rule itself is your and Shakeb's call, but I wouldn't do it this close to the review.

supabase/, apps/mobile or packages/.

## Important: Codex is working in parallel

Codex is currently doing a structural change on its own branch
(chore/shared-packages): a root workspace, packages/contract (API types
generated from the FastAPI schema) and packages/brand (design tokens from
DESIGN.md), with the frontend switched over to use them. Then it builds an
Expo app in apps/mobile.

To avoid conflicts, this task has two phases:
- **Phase A (now): review only. No code changes, no branch.** Read, audit,
  screenshot, and report.
- **Phase B: fixes, only after I tell you chore/shared-packages is merged.**
  Then fetch, branch from the latest origin/main, and build on the new
  structure: types from packages/contract, tokens from packages/brand.

## Workflow for Phase B (as always)

- git fetch, then a branch from the latest origin/main for each chunk.
- You write the code. You never commit, push or merge.
- Tests first for behaviour changes. After each chunk, give me the
  `git add` command (always including .claude-logs/, never env files) and a
  commit message.
- Before each PR: bring in the latest origin/main (I'll run the merge), rerun
  lint, typecheck and tests, then open the PR. Don't merge.

## 1. Feedback we've received so far

From feedback session 1: the reviewers suggested a mobile app instead of a
web app, because tracking every day on the web is hard. That's being built
separately in Codex (an Expo app for the daily loop), so don't build any
mobile work here.

But consider what else that feedback implies for the web app:
- what in the web daily loop makes tracking feel like a chore today (steps
  to reach today's check-in, sign-in friction, slow loading, no reason to
  come back), with evidence from our code and UI
- two or three small changes that would reduce that friction on the web
  too, smallest first, and which you'd do first and why

## 2. Make the UI unmistakably ours, not AI-default

Audit every screen against the patterns that make a UI look AI-generated:
- default fonts and type scales with no real hierarchy
- gradients, glows or glass used as decoration
- the same rounded card with the same shadow everywhere
- emoji or generic icon sets standing in for real visuals
- generic copy ("Unlock your potential", "Transform your health") instead of
  plain, specific words about sleep, food and movement
- everything centred, everything the same size, nothing leading the eye
- motion on everything, instead of motion that answers an action

For each one you find: the screen, file and line, why it reads as generic,
and the fix, consistent with DESIGN.md. Compare with
https://www.karma.business/ for the quality bar, not the look. Include
screenshots.

Also list which of these rules should be added to DESIGN.md, so the mobile
app follows them too.

## 3. Architecture and code quality (scored automatically)

Review the frontend code as a senior reviewer would:
- structure: components, data layer, types, where logic lives
- duplication, dead code, leftover sample data or mocks in the real flow

The Phase A review is done and nothing in the repo changed. There's no branch or file edit, and `git status` shows only the session logs. Codex appears to share this folder: a Codex log was added during the review, so I didn't switch branches.

To get screenshots without asking you to sign in, I used the existing layout test, which mocks a signed-in account, and moved its images out of the repo straight away. The five screenshots I sent are the evidence for the items below.

## 1. What makes the web daily loop feel like a chore

| Friction | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **Nothing brings you back.** | There are no reminders, notifications, calendar file or streak anywhere in `app/`, `components/` or `lib/`. The only prompt is the text "Day 2 opens Friday 5:29 AM" (`experiment-progress.tsx:76`), and it only appears if you return on your own. |
| **Slow first paint of Your lab.** | `home-experience.tsx:43–55` waits for the session, then makes three requests one after another: profile, then the active experiment, then its check-ins. That's three round trips before anything shows. I didn't measure live, because the pane was signed out. |
| **Two hops to answer.** | Your lab → **Check in for day N** → a separate page that loads the experiment and check-ins again (`checkin-form.tsx:80`) → answer → save → **Back to your experiment**. |
| **Scales without meaning.** | 1–10 buttons with no words at either end (`metric-input.tsx:70–97`), so "6" means different things on different days. That makes every check-in slower and the data noisier. |
| **Sign-in.** | Low friction: the session persists, and magic link was skipped for V1 by decision. Not a priority. |

**Smallest fixes first:**
1. **Add to calendar on "Done for today".** A client-side `.ics` file with "Day N+1 opens …" at the right time. One small component, no backend. This is the closest the web gets to a reminder.
2. **Load Your lab in parallel.** Fetch the profile and the active experiment at the same time, which cuts one round trip.
3. **A `/today` link.** It redirects to today's check-in, or to Your lab if you're done or nothing is active. One address people can bookmark or add to their phone's home screen.

**Do first: 1.** It's the only fix that gives people a reason to come back on day 3 and day 7, which is exactly the problem the reviewers raised.

## 2. AI-default patterns found

The base is already strong. There are no gradients or decorative shadows, no icon set and no emoji, real fonts (Fraunces, Instrument Sans, Geist Mono), and motion only answers an action or the scroll. What's left is smaller:

| # | Screen | Where | Why it reads as generic | Fix (consistent with DESIGN.md) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Your lab, experiment page | `experiment-progress.tsx:196`, rendered in a fixed-height empty box | A large empty panel saying "Choose a recorded day…" (screenshots 1 and 2). Nothing leads the eye, and the page looks unfinished. | Show the latest recorded day by default, or collapse the space until a day is chosen. |
| D2 | Experiment page | `experiment-detail.tsx`; `rounded-card` is used 93 times across `components/` | Five cards in the same shape (screenshot 2), so everything has the same weight. | Keep cards for the decision (today's check-in) and the comparison (forecast). Make "The small change" and the timeline plain sections with dividers, as DESIGN.md says. |
| D3 | Your lab | `home-experience.tsx:111` (footer nav), `:74` and `:152` ("Your lab" label) | The same three links appear in the header and again in the footer, plus a "Your lab" label right under the "Your lab" tab. | Remove the repeated nav. Keep "Update your rhythm" in the account menu, where it already is. |
| D4 | Ask a question, history, experiment, report | `question-form.tsx:31`, `experiment-history.tsx:94`, `experiment-flow.tsx:115`, `experiment-detail.tsx:168` | Mono "eyebrow" labels with words, like "A little curiosity, a little evidence", above headings: a common template pattern. DESIGN.md keeps mono for times and counts. | Keep mono only for numbers ("Day 4 of 10"). Drop the word eyebrows or set them in Instrument Sans. |
| D5 | Check-in | `metric-input.tsx:70–97` | An unlabelled 1–10 grid (screenshot 3) looks like a generic number pad. | Add words for each end, such as "1 drained · 10 full of energy". These are frontend copy keyed by metric, with a neutral "low / high" fallback for metrics the AI invents. A backend field would be better; that's a request for Shakeb. |
| D6 | Several | `experiment-history.tsx:103` "Your next discovery starts with a question."; `experiment-detail.tsx:215` "Results unlock soon."; `question-form.tsx:36` "Let's turn it into something you can test"; `experiment-flow.tsx:477` "Start with a hunch"; `sign-in.tsx:36` "Welcome back." | Generic product-speak or game language. | Use plain, specific words, for example "Results after 2 normal days and 3 test days", "No finished experiments yet. Your first one takes 10 days.", "Sign in to log today's check-in." |
| D7 | Onboarding | `onboarding-flow.tsx:185` (Start pushed right with `ml-auto`), `:199` (empty ring) | An empty placeholder clock dominates the first screen (screenshot 5), and the main action floats to the right. | Put Start under the text, left-aligned. Show the ring faint and labelled "fills in as you answer", or only after the first answer. |
| D8 | Check-in and onboarding phone bars | `checkin-form.tsx:391`, `onboarding-flow.tsx:170` (`backdrop-blur`) | Frosted glass, the one decorative effect left. | A solid `bg-oat` with the top border. |

**Rules to add to DESIGN.md**, so the mobile app follows them too:
- **No placeholders as content:** an empty panel never takes more than one line.
- **Cards only for a decision or a comparison;** information uses sections and dividers.
- **Mono only for numbers, times and counts.**
- **Every scale names both ends in plain words.**
- **No glass, gradients, glows or decorative shadows.**
- **No navigation repeated on the same screen.**
- **Copy says what will happen, with real numbers.** No "unlock", "journey" or "discover".

## 3. Code review, ranked

| # | Problem | File | Risk | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | API responses are never checked at runtime | `lib/api/client.ts:68` (`return body as T`) | If the backend changes shape, the UI breaks quietly instead of showing an error. The contract test only checks our fixtures. | Validate key responses against the generated contract. **After chore/shared-packages.** |
| C2 | Button styles copied into 9 files as 14 local constants, and they've drifted | `home-experience.tsx:22`, `experiment-history.tsx:21`, `experiment-progress.tsx:11`, `experiment-detail.tsx:19`, `new-experiment.tsx:14`, `lab-report.tsx:28`, `experiment-flow.tsx:15`, `checkin-form.tsx:29`… | Heights (44 or 48px), transitions and press feedback differ between screens. | One shared button style. **After chore/shared-packages** (brand tokens). |
| C3 | Dead code and an unused sample profile | `components/auth-panel.tsx` and `components/backend-status.tsx` (0 importers); `lib/data/profile.ts:23` `exampleProfile` and `lib/mock/profile.ts` (unused since the new landing page) | Confusing to reviewers, and mock data sits next to the real flow. | Delete them and update `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §6, which still describes the sample profile. Safe now. |
| C4 | Two `signedPercent` functions that round differently | `experiment-flow.tsx:18` vs `lib/report/copy.ts:51` (R9) | The same forecast can read "+7.5%" on the proposal and "+8%" on the report. | Use the one in `copy.ts`, with a test. Safe now. |
| C5 | Very large components | `experiment-flow.tsx` (486 lines), `lab-report.tsx` (471), `checkin-form.tsx` (467) | Harder to review. | Move `LearningSection` and `NextExperimentSection` out of `lab-report.tsx`, with the existing tests unchanged. Only if time allows. |
| C6 | Your lab requests run in sequence | `home-experience.tsx:43–55` | Slow first paint (section 1). | Fetch them in parallel, with a test. Safe now. |

There's no `any` and no `as unknown as` anywhere in `app/`, `components/` or `lib/`. Error handling goes through a single `ApiError`. Apart from C3, the code matches `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

## Combined list: what to fix first for the demo

1. **D1 and D3: remove the empty day box and the repeated nav on Your lab and the experiment page.** These are the first screens in the demo, and both fixes are small.
2. **Calendar reminder on "Done for today".** It answers the reviewers' "tracking every day is hard" directly on the web.
3. **D5: name both ends of each check-in scale.** This is the daily action, so it's seen every day. Shakeb's input on the scale words would help.
4. **D6 and D4: replace the generic copy and the mono word labels.** Mostly text changes, but they're what makes it read as ours.
5. **C3 and C4: delete the dead code and the duplicate formatter.** Visible in the automatic code score, and close to zero risk.
6. **C6: load Your lab in parallel.**
7. **D2, D7, D8: card hierarchy, onboarding layout, the glass bars.**
8. **After chore/shared-packages:** C1 (response checks) and C2 (shared button style).
9. **C5:** only if there's time.

I'll wait here until you tell me chore/shared-packages is merged. It isn't on the remote yet.

## Handover for your update

**What changed and why:**
- After today's check-in, the "Done for today" card offers **Add day N+1 to your calendar**, so coming back tomorrow doesn't depend on remembering. This answers the reviewers' "tracking every day on the web is hard".
- The event is built in the browser. It starts exactly when the next rolling day opens, alerts at that time, and links straight back to the check-in. There's no backend change and no calendar account access.
- It names only the day, never the habit question, because calendars are often shared. Adding it twice updates the same event instead of making two.

**Issues hit and how they were solved:**
- Calendar files must fold lines longer than 75 bytes, so the check-in URL splits across lines. The tests unfold before checking contents.
- The test browser can't read a downloaded file's text the usual way, so the unit test reads it with `FileReader`, and a real Chromium download proves the end-to-end path.
- Codex was using the shared test port, so I waited instead of stopping its run.

**Demo script, under 2 minutes:**
1. Sign in at phone width and open **Your lab** with today's check-in done. The card reads "Done for today · Day 4 is recorded · Day 5 opens Friday 2:00 PM."
2. Tap **Add day 5 to your calendar**, then open the downloaded file. It goes into Calendar at the exact time day 5 opens.
3. Point at the event title: "Rhythm check-in: day 5 of 10". It doesn't mention the habit.
4. When the alert fires, tap its link, which goes straight to day 5's check-in.

> **Why it matters:** "You don't have to remember to come back: your calendar reminds you the moment tomorrow's check-in opens, and one tap takes you there."

**Next:** C3 + C4, removing the dead code and the duplicate percent formatter, from a fresh branch once this PR is open.
